a9c3e5b23fa3f4ef3 toolu_017h4URuyQWYo3dcm9GHbuzJ /tmp/claude-1000/-mnt-D-py-cascade-cms/bbddc9ff-dca4-488c-a40e-96e584e0085b/tasks/a9c3e5b23fa3f4ef3.output completed Agent "Explore cascade_cms library internals" finished A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once. I now have complete grounding. Here is my full report. ## 1. `Operations` class — `search`, `read`, `listSites` File: `/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/operations.py` `Operations` is defined at **line 964** (`@dataclass class Operations`, fields `_driver`, `_chains`, `_logger` at 990-992). Every method below **starts a new chain and returns immediately** — no network call happens until `CascadeWrapperBase.submit_requests()` (in `wrapper.py`) runs. There is a lower-level, single-verb counterpart on `OperationChain` (used internally / for chaining) at lines 240 (`read`), 429 (`search`), 471 (`listSites`). - **`Operations.read`** — line 1022: ```python def read( self, identifiers: IdentifierType | Path | list[IdentifierType | Path], parser=parse_assets, ) -&gt; OperationChain | ChainGroup: ``` A single identifier returns an `OperationChain`; a **list** of identifiers fans out into a `ChainGroup` of independent chains (one per identifier, "Approach A", lines 1004-1016), so a batch failure is reported per-asset. Underlying `OperationChain.read` (line 240) issues `GET read/{type}/{id-or-path}`. - **`Operations.search`** — line 1102: ```python def search( self, payload: SearchInformation, parser=parse_list_elements, ) -&gt; OperationChain: ``` Always a single chain (no list-of-payloads fan-out). `OperationChain.search` (line 429) asserts `isinstance(payload, SearchInformation)` and does `POST search`. `SearchInformation` (cmstypes.py line 799) fields: `siteName: str`, `searchTerms: str`, `searchFields: list[FieldsSearchTypes] | list[Literal[""]]` (default `[""]`), `searchTypes: list[AssetTypes] | list[Literal[""]]` (default `[""]`). **There is no `limit`/page-size field anywhere in this payload** — the plan's `limit: int = 20` param has no server-side counterpart; it would have to be applied by the MCP layer by truncating `ListElements.elements` client-side, and `has_more`/`total_count` in the plan's proposed return shape can't be sourced from Cascade at all (Cascade's raw list responses carry no total count). - **`Operations.listSites`** — line 1136: ```python def listSites(self, parser=parse_list_elements) -&gt; OperationChain: ``` No params. `OperationChain.listSites` (line 471) issues `GET listSites`. **Return shape** — none of these return raw dicts. `submit_requests()` (wrapper.py line 90) unwraps each request down to `ResponseParser._content` (driver.py lines 123 &amp; 146: `return parsed_response._content`), so the final value per chain is always a **typed Pydantic object**, never a bare dict: - `read` → `Asset` (success) or `CascadeError` (failure) - `search`/`listSites` → `ListElements` (success) or `CascadeError` (failure) `ListElements` (cmstypes.py line 760): `elements: list[IdentifierType | Message | Audit]`, aliased from whichever JSON key the endpoint actually uses (`AliasChoices("preferences","matches","messages","relationships","sites")` — `"matches"` for search, `"sites"` for listSites). `.flat` property (777) is just `self.elements`. Each `IdentifierType` element (line 342) exposes `.get_id`, `.get_type`, `.get_path`, `.get_sitename`, `.get_site_id` properties (366-390) — there is **no name/title field** anywhere on it, confirming the plan's note in §6.1 that a pseudo-name must be derived from the last path segment. **Error signaling**: not exceptions for API-level failures. `ResponseParser.__init__` (cmstypes.py line 1004) tries `CascadeError.model_validate_json(raw)` first; only on `ValidationError` does it fall through to the real `serializer`. So a Cascade `{"success": false, "message": "..."}` response becomes a `CascadeError` object (line 781: `success: bool = False`, `message: str = ""`) returned **as the chain's result value**, not raised. `submit_requests()` explicitly documents this (wrapper.py lines 101-105): "Failures appear in that list rather than being dropped... They are returned as values, not raised — a missed `isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception)` check lets a failure flow downstream as if it were a normal result." Python exceptions (e.g. a callback raising) are also captured as values, not propagated, for the same reason — an MCP wrapper must explicitly `isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception)`-check every result and translate that into a `ToolError`, exactly as the plan's §6.1/§6.6 assume. ## 2. `Asset` class — `cmstypes.py` Defined at **line 555** (class docstring: "Dynamic wrapper around a raw Cascade asset JSON payload"). **`__init__`** (line 572): ```python def __init__(self, data: dict): object.__setattr__(self, "_asset_type", next(iter(data["asset"].keys()))) inner: dict[str, Any] = data["asset"][self._asset_type] object.__setattr__(self, "_data", inner) if "pageConfigurations" in self._data: object.__setattr__(self, "_page_configs", [PageConfiguration(**config) for config in self._data["pageConfigurations"]]) else: object.__setattr__(self, "_page_configs", []) ``` - `_asset_type` = raw response wrapper key (e.g. `"dataDefinition"`, as-cased by Cascade). - `_data` = the inner asset dict **by reference** (the actual field bag — `id`, `path`, `name`, `structuredData`, `pageConfigurations`, etc., varies per type). - `_page_configs` = eagerly parsed `list[PageConfiguration]` from `_data["pageConfigurations"]` if present, else `[]`. - `.asset_type` property (612) normalizes `_asset_type.lower()` through `_ASSET_TYPE_KEY_ALIASES` (608: only `"scriptformat" → "format"` currently mapped) — this is the request-side type string matching `AssetTypes`/`Path.asset_type`. **`get_data_structure`** (line 625): ```python def get_data_structure(self: 'Asset', group: str, identifier: str) -&gt; list[dict[str, Any]] | None: ``` Internal `find_group(obj)` (632) is a **recursive generator walk over the entire `self._data` tree** (dicts and lists), `yield`ing any dict where `obj.get("type") == "group" and obj.get("identifier") == group` — i.e. it matches on `type == "group"` exactly as you suspected, keyed additionally by the group's own `identifier` value. For each matched group node, `find_in_nodes` (642) recursively descends `structuredDataNodes` looking for a leaf node (`"structuredDataNodes" not in node`) whose `"identifier"` equals the target `identifier` param, returning the **first leaf match per group instance**. Results across all group instances are collected into `matches`; returns `matches or None`. **Uncommitted change** (see §4 below): this now raises `KeyError` if a node dict is missing `"identifier"` or a group node is missing `"structuredDataNodes"` — previously `.get()` calls silently tolerated absence. A **true "not found"** (fields present but no matching identifier) still returns `None`, not an exception — so the MCP plan's §6.3 "not found → `ToolError` listing available group names" needs to handle **both** a `None` return (no match) and a possible `KeyError` (malformed instance data) as distinct failure modes. **`get_page_configuration`** (line 670): ```python def get_page_configuration( self, configuration_name: str, page_region: str | None = None ) -&gt; PageConfiguration | PageRegion | None: ``` Looks up `next((c for c in self._page_configs if c.name == configuration_name), None)`; if `page_region` given, further looks up `next((r for r in config.pageRegions if r.name == page_region), None)`. Returns `None` on no match (either level) — Pydantic model objects by reference otherwise, never a dict (matches the plan's §6.4 expectation to `.model_dump()` before returning over MCP). `PageConfiguration`/`PageRegion` (lines 456-473) **are Pydantic `BaseModel`s**, not TypedDicts/plain dicts: ```python class PageRegion(BaseModel): model_config = ConfigDict(validate_assignment=True, from_attributes=True) name: str content: str | None = None class PageConfiguration(BaseModel): model_config = ConfigDict(validate_assignment=True, from_attributes=True) name: str pageRegions: list[PageRegion] ``` Note the raw JSON's `pageConfigurations[i]` entries also carry `templateId`/`blockId`/`formatId` (confirmed in `tests/test_cmstypes.py` line 108-128 fixture and `AssetAdapter.dump_json`'s comment at cmstypes.py line 961-966) — **these are not modeled fields**; `PageConfiguration` only parses `name`+`pageRegions`, so `.model_dump()` on it will **not** include `templateId`/`blockId`/`formatId` even though they're present in `_data["pageConfigurations"]`. If the MCP tool wants those IDs (likely useful for §7's "resolve the bound format/template asset" follow-up), it needs to read them from `Asset._data["pageConfigurations"]` directly, not from `_page_configs`. **`root_container_id`** (line 716): ```python def root_container_id(self, asset_type: "AssetTypes") -&gt; uuid.UUID | None: ``` Looks up `_ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS.get(asset_type)`; if unmapped returns `None` (not an error); if mapped but the site's `_data` lacks that field, also `None`; otherwise `uuid.UUID(value)`. `_ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS` (`ClassVar[dict[str,str]]`, line 710): ```python _ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS: ClassVar[dict[str, str]] = { "datadefinition": "rootDataDefinitionContainerId", "sharedfield": "rootSharedFieldContainerId", "folder": "rootFolderId", } ``` Exactly the three types named in the plan §6.5 — no others confirmed. Docstring explicitly notes unmapped types are unmapped by design ("Only asset types confirmed against a real site payload are listed here; unmapped types return None rather than guess"), so the plan's "unmapped `asset_type` → `ToolError`" (§6.5) must be enforced in the MCP layer, since the library itself is silent (`None`) on both "unmapped type" and "site has no such field" — those two failure modes are **indistinguishable from library output alone**, another thing worth flagging in the plan. **`IdentifierType | Path` union** — cmstypes.py: - `PathBase` (TypedDict, line 200): `path: str`, `siteId: NotRequired[uuid.UUID]`, `siteName: str | None = None`. - `Path(PathBase)` (line 216): adds `asset_type: Literal[AssetTypes]`. - `IdentifierType` (BaseModel, line 342, `frozen=True, extra="forbid"`): `identifier: uuid.UUID` (alias `"id"`), `asset_type: AssetTypes` (alias `"type"`), `recycled: bool | None = None`, `path: PathBase | None = None`. Properties `.get_id`/`.get_type`/`.get_path`/`.get_sitename`/`.get_site_id` (366-390). - `resolve_identifier(identifier)` (line 412) turns either into the URL path segments: `IdentifierType` → `(asset_type, id)`; `Path` → `(asset_type, siteName, path)`, **raising `ValueError`** if a `Path` lacks `siteName` (line 421-422). ## 3. `CascadeWrapperBase` and construction pattern Defined in **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/wrapper.py`**, class at line 23, `__init__` at line 36. ```python class EnvironmentVars(TypedDict): # wrapper.py:15 SERVER: str API_KEY: str CASCADE_URL: str def __init__( self, environmentVariables: EnvironmentVars, configurationVariables: dict[str, Any] | None, debug: dict[str, Any] | None = None, ): ``` **Important correction to the plan's §4 assumption**: `CascadeWrapperBase` does **not** read `os.environ` itself and has no built-in "env var names" — it's a plain constructor taking a dict. There is no single canonical OS env var name baked into the library; each caller wires `os.environ` into the `EnvironmentVars` dict themselves. However, every actual example in the repo (the skill's bundled templates, unpacked from `skill/cascade-script-writer-lite-311.skill` → `templates/read-identifiers.py`, `create-bulk.py`, `edit-in-place.py`, `callback-none.py`, `workflow-orchestration.py`, and consistent with `README.md` lines 11-22) uses the **same** convention: ```python environment_variables = { "API_KEY": os.environ["CASCADE_API_KEY"], "CASCADE_URL": os.environ["CASCADE_URL"], "SERVER": os.environ.get("SERVER", "default"), } configuration_variables = { "cache_name": "./cache/cache.sqlite", "allowed_codes": (200,), "allowed_methods": ("GET",), } with CascadeWrapperBase(environment_variables, configuration_variables) as cascade: ... ``` **This means the MCP plan's §3 client-config example is wrong**: it says `CASCADE_API_URL` — the repo-wide convention (README + all 5 skill templates) is **`CASCADE_URL`** (paired with `CASCADE_API_KEY` for the key, which the plan does get right). This should be fixed before implementation — `CASCADE_API_URL` doesn't exist anywhere else in the codebase. `configurationVariables` is forwarded essentially verbatim to `aiohttp_client_cache.SQLiteBackend(**backendConfig)` (driver.py lines 212-215); `None` uses `default_cache_backend()` (driver.py line 149-160, same shape: `cache_name="./cache/cache.sqlite", allowed_codes=(200,), allowed_methods=("GET",)`). `__exit__` (line 70) re-raises any exception from inside the `with` block except `RuntimeWarning`. `submit_requests()` (line 90) is the only network trigger, batches all registered chains concurrently, and always clears `self.operations._chains` afterward (line 140), whether it succeeded or not — so a fresh `Operations` call is needed per batch. Also relevant: `/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/tests/edit_test.py` is a **manual, human-run** live-server script (excluded from pytest collection and from ruff per `pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.ruff] exclude` comment), showing the same `EnvironmentVars(...)` construction pattern but with hardcoded literal values rather than `os.environ`. ## 4. Existing MCP-related prep work already in the repo - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`** (untracked, new) — this **is** the implementation plan itself already checked into the working tree (not yet committed). No other call site in the repo passed a `default` arg, so this is a clean/compatible change. - `Asset.get_data_structure` (line 625) and `Asset.get_page_configuration` (line 670) now wrap their internal dict/attribute access in `try/except KeyError` and **re-raise `KeyError`** with a descriptive message when required fields (`identifier`, `structuredDataNodes`, `name`, `pageRegions`) are missing from a node/model — previously these used tolerant `.get()` calls that would silently skip malformed nodes. - `identifier_from_asset` (line 426) was changed to read `siteName`/`siteId` via `asset._data.get(...)` directly (bypassing the now-strict `Asset.get`, since those two are legitimately optional), while `path`/`id` still go through the now-strict `asset.get(...)` and will raise `KeyError` if absent — the docstring's earlier caveat about "does not itself guard against a missing id" was removed since it now guards via the strict `.get()`. - `OperationChain._edit_identifier` (operations.py ~line 339) updated to catch the new `KeyError` from `asset.get("id")` and re-raise its existing descriptive `ValueError`, falling back to `asset._data.get("path", "&lt;unknown&gt;")` for the error message (previously used the old tolerant `.get()`). - **`tests/test_cmstypes.py`** has matching new/updated tests, notably `test_identifier_from_asset_missing_id_raises` (line 212) which asserts `identifier_from_asset` now raises `KeyError` (not a bare `UUID(None)` `TypeError`) on a missing `id`, and `test_asset_root_container_id_known_and_unknown_types` (line 221) which pins down exactly the `_ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS` behavior described above. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once. # Phase 1 Implementation Plan — `cascade-cms-rest-mcp` (Read Backbone) This plan is grounded in `/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (read in full) plus fresh verification against the actual repo (`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms`) and the actual `mcp` PyPI package (downloaded and inspected — see §0). Phase 2 is explicitly out of scope pending a real `structuredData`/`pageConfigurations` example. --- ## 0. Critical findings that change/refine the locked doc These aren't redesigns of the tool specs — they're implementation facts the locked doc couldn't have had, discovered by reading the actual code and the actual `mcp` package (v2.1.1, the current PyPI release as of today, downloaded and inspected directly since it isn't installed locally): 1. **`mcp` SDK is now v2.x, and `FastMCP` was renamed.** `pip index versions mcp` → latest is `2.1.1` (last 1.x was `1.29.1`). I downloaded `mcp-2.1.1` and confirmed: - `mcp/server/fastmcp.py` (the old v1 import path) is now a **stub that raises `ModuleNotFoundError`** on import, with a message pointing at the v2 migration guide. - The real class is `MCPServer` at `mcp.server.mcpserver.MCPServer` (drop-in conceptual replacement for `FastMCP`). - `ToolError` lives at `mcp.server.mcpserver.exceptions.ToolError` (not re-exported from the package `__init__`). - The locked doc's `mcp&gt;=1.0.0` dependency pin, unpinned, would resolve to `2.1.1` today and then **fail at import** with the old `from mcp.server.fastmcp import FastMCP` pattern the doc assumed. **This must be corrected to target the v2 API and pin `mcp&gt;=2.1.0,&lt;3`** (see §5). 2. **Sync tool functions already run off the event-loop thread — no manual `asyncio.to_thread` needed.** `CascadeCMSRestDriver.__init__` (`driver.py:202-203`) does `self.eventLoop = asyncio.new_event_loop(); asyncio.set_event_loop(self.eventLoop)` and drives everything via `run_until_complete` — i.e. `CascadeWrapperBase`/`Operations`/`submit_requests()` is a **fully synchronous, blocking API that owns its own private event loop**, incompatible with being called from inside a coroutine already running on the same thread. I confirmed in `mcp/server/mcpserver/utilities/func_metadata.py` that `MCPServer`'s tool dispatch already does exactly the right thing for this: *"A sync function runs on a worker thread"* — `await anyio.to_thread.run_sync(functools.partial(fn, **kwargs))`. So Phase 1 tool functions should be **plain synchronous `def`s** that do the whole `with CascadeWrapperBase(...) as cascade: ...` block directly — the SDK offloads them to a worker thread automatically. This resolves what would otherwise be a real bug risk. 3. **`@server.tool()` returns the original function unchanged.** Confirmed by reading `server.py`'s `tool()` decorator: `def decorator(fn): self.add_tool(fn, ...); return fn`. This means `cascade_search`/`cascade_read_asset` remain ordinary, directly-callable Python functions after decoration — a big simplification for both unit tests and the manual smoke test (call them directly, no protocol harness needed). 4. **`submit_requests()` can return an empty list even when a chain was registered**, if a top-level exception occurs (`wrapper.py`: `except Exception as e: ...; return []`). Destructuring `[result] = cascade.submit_requests()` naively would then raise a raw `ValueError`, violating "never raised as bare exceptions." §3.4 below adds an explicit guard for this. 5. **Default cache path is CWD-relative** (`driver.py:157`, `./cache/cache.sqlite`), constructed on demand. For an MCP server launched by `uvx`/a desktop client from an unpredictable working directory, this would scatter cache dirs. `config.py` should supply an explicit, stable cache location (§2.2). 6. **The locked doc's `CASCADE_API_URL` env var name doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase** — confirmed by grep; the real name (used by every skill template and `README.md`) is `CASCADE_URL`, paired with `CASCADE_API_KEY`. Already flagged by you; this plan fixes it in `config.py` and the client-config example. 7. **Top-level package (`src/cascade_cms/__init__.py`) does not, and must not, import `.mcp`.** Confirmed by reading it — it only imports `driver`/`operation_logger`/`operations`/`wrapper`. This is what makes "base `cascade-cms-rest` install unaffected" true *by construction*, not just by promise — I'll call this out as a guardrail so nobody "helpfully" adds `from . import mcp` later. 8. **`structuredData` as the top-level key name is inferred, not confirmed in this codebase.** Grep for `structuredData` (outside of `structuredDataNodes`) returns zero hits anywhere in `src/` or `tests/` — there is no fixture proving Cascade's read response actually uses that literal key. `pageConfigurations`, by contrast, *is* confirmed (used directly in `Asset.__init__`). Phase 1's collapse-routing logic needs *a* key name to special-case for the `expand_with: "cascade_get_data_structure"` hint even though that tool doesn't ship until Phase 2 — I'm using `"structuredData"` per Cascade's documented REST field name, but flagging it as a cheap, independent thing to confirm against one real `format="detailed"` response from your dev site during Phase 1 smoke testing (this does *not* block Phase 1 — worst case the hint just doesn't fire for one field name and falls through to the generic `cascade_read_asset(format="detailed")` hint, which is still correct, just less specific). None of this changes tool signatures, params, or response shapes from §6.1/§6.2 of the locked doc — it's all implementation substrate. --- ## 1. Directory layout for this round ``` src/cascade_cms/mcp/ ├── __init__.py # docstring only, no re-exports (see finding #7) ├── config.py # env var loading + cache path config ├── errors.py # CascadeError/Exception -&gt; ToolError translation ├── formatting.py # concise/detailed collapse, name-from-path, search shaping └── server.py # MCPServer instance, 2 tools, main() tests/mcp/ ├── conftest.py # collect_ignore for smoke_test.py (mirrors tests/conftest.py) ├── test_formatting.py # pytest-collected, no live server ├── test_errors.py # pytest-collected, no live server ├── test_config.py # pytest-collected, no live server ├── test_server.py # pytest-collected, mocked driver (stretch goal, see §4.4) └── smoke_test.py # manual, human-run against a real dev site ``` --- ## 2. `config.py` ```python """Environment/config wiring for the MCP server, matching CascadeWrapperBase's existing expectations exactly - no new credential-naming surface. """ from __future__ import annotations import os from pathlib import Path from cascade_cms.wrapper import EnvironmentVars # The two hard requirements. SERVER is cosmetic (log-file naming) and # defaults, matching every existing skill template's convention. _REQUIRED = ("CASCADE_API_KEY", "CASCADE_URL") def load_environment_variables() -&gt; EnvironmentVars: """Read credentials from os.environ, or raise SystemExit naming exactly what's missing. Called at server startup (main()), before mcp.run() - fail fast, not on first tool call. """ missing = [name for name in _REQUIRED if not os.environ.get(name)] if missing: raise SystemExit( "cascade-cms-rest-mcp: missing required environment variable(s): " + ", ".join(missing) + ". Set these in your MCP client's `env` block " '(e.g. {"CASCADE_API_KEY": "...", "CASCADE_URL": "https://your-cascade-host"}).' ) return EnvironmentVars( API_KEY=os.environ["CASCADE_API_KEY"], CASCADE_URL=os.environ["CASCADE_URL"], SERVER=os.environ.get("SERVER", "default"), ) def cache_configuration() -&gt; dict[str, object]: """`configurationVariables` for CascadeWrapperBase. Explicit path rather than the library's CWD-relative `./cache/cache.sqlite` default (driver.py's default_cache_backend()), since the MCP client launches this process from an arbitrary/unpredictable working directory (uvx, Claude Desktop, etc.) - a relative path would scatter cache dirs. Override with CASCADE_MCP_CACHE_DIR for advanced use / testing. """ cache_dir = Path(os.environ.get("CASCADE_MCP_CACHE_DIR", Path.home() / ".cache" / "cascade-cms-mcp")) cache_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True) return { "cache_name": str(cache_dir / "cache.sqlite"), "allowed_codes": (200,), "allowed_methods": ("GET",), } ``` Notes: - Uses `EnvironmentVars` (the `TypedDict` already defined in `wrapper.py`) as the return type rather than a bespoke type — zero duplication. - `SystemExit` (not a bare `raise Exception`) is deliberate: this fires from `main()` before `mcp.run()` starts, i.e. before there's any MCP session to report a `ToolError` into — process exit with a clear stderr message is the right failure mode here, matching how CLI tools fail on bad config. --- ## 3. `errors.py` ```python """Translate Cascade-level failures (CascadeError values, or raw exceptions that leaked out of submit_requests) into self-correcting MCP ToolErrors. Never let a bare Python exception escape a tool body - see server.py. """ from __future__ import annotations from typing import Any from cascade_cms.cmstypes import CascadeError, IdentifierType, Path from mcp.server.mcpserver.exceptions import ToolError def describe_identifier(identifier: IdentifierType | Path) -&gt; str: if isinstance(identifier, IdentifierType): return f"{identifier.get_type} {identifier.get_id}" return f"{identifier['asset_type']} at {identifier.get('siteName')}:{identifier['path']}" def single_result(results: list[Any], *, context: str) -&gt; Any: """Unwrap a one-chain submit_requests() result, raising a ToolError instead of a bare ValueError if Cascade returned nothing at all (a top-level submit_requests() failure returns [] - see wrapper.py). """ if not results: raise ToolError( f"{context}: no response was received from Cascade - this usually means a " "connectivity or session-level failure rather than a bad request. Check " "CASCADE_URL and CASCADE_API_KEY, confirm the server is reachable, and retry." ) return results[0] def search_failure_error(result: CascadeError | Exception) -&gt; ToolError: if isinstance(result, CascadeError): message = result.message or "Cascade reported a search failure with no further detail." return ToolError(f"cascade_search failed: {message}") return ToolError( f"cascade_search failed unexpectedly ({result}). This usually indicates a " "connectivity/credential problem rather than a bad query - check CASCADE_URL " "and CASCADE_API_KEY." ) def read_asset_error(identifier: IdentifierType | Path, result: CascadeError | Exception) -&gt; ToolError: described = describe_identifier(identifier) if isinstance(result, CascadeError): message = result.message or "asset not found" return ToolError( f"cascade_read_asset failed for {described}: {message}. " "Try `cascade_search` first to confirm the correct id/type/path, then retry " "cascade_read_asset with its result." ) return ToolError( f"cascade_read_asset failed unexpectedly for {described} ({result}). This usually " "indicates a connectivity/credential problem - check CASCADE_URL and CASCADE_API_KEY." ) def unexpected_failure_error(tool_name: str, exc: Exception) -&gt; ToolError: """Last-resort translation for anything not already a ToolError, so no tool body can ever let a raw traceback leak to the client. """ return ToolError(f"{tool_name} failed unexpectedly: {exc}") ``` This satisfies the acceptance criterion literally: every message names what was tried, what's actually available/what to check, and/or which tool to call next — never a bare "not found." --- ## 4. `formatting.py` ```python """Concise/detailed asset formatting and search-result shaping. Reaches into Asset._data directly (a leading-underscore attribute) - Asset has no public bulk-iteration API, and the library's own identifier_from_asset() (cmstypes.py) already does the same thing, so this is precedented, not a hack. """ from __future__ import annotations import uuid from typing import Any, Literal from cascade_cms.cmstypes import Asset, IdentifierType, ListElements # Locked routing rule (MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md §6.2). "structuredData" is # Cascade's documented REST field name for data-bound asset types, but is NOT # yet confirmed against a real payload in this repo (no fixture references # it) - unlike "pageConfigurations", which Asset.__init__ already parses. # Sanity-check this key name against one real cascade_read_asset(format="detailed") # response during Phase 1 smoke testing; if wrong, only that one hint's # specificity is affected (falls through to the generic detailed-mode hint). _EXPAND_HINT_BY_KEY: dict[str, str] = { "structuredData": "cascade_get_data_structure", "pageConfigurations": "cascade_get_page_config", } _DEFAULT_EXPAND_HINT = 'cascade_read_asset(format="detailed")' _SCALAR_TYPES = (str, int, float, bool, type(None)) def _collapse_value(key: str, value: Any) -&gt; Any: if isinstance(value, _SCALAR_TYPES): return value if isinstance(value, uuid.UUID): return str(value) if isinstance(value, (dict, list)): return { "_collapsed": True, "count": len(value), "expand_with": _EXPAND_HINT_BY_KEY.get(key, _DEFAULT_EXPAND_HINT), } # Raw JSON only ever produces the types above; stringify rather than # crash on anything unforeseen instead of letting a KeyError-shaped # surprise escape formatting. return str(value) def format_asset(asset: Asset, *, format: Literal["concise", "detailed"]) -&gt; dict[str, Any]: if format == "detailed": return dict(asset._data) return {key: _collapse_value(key, value) for key, value in asset._data.items()} def name_from_path(path: str | None) -&gt; str | None: """Derive a pseudo display-name from an asset's path - Cascade search results carry no name/title field of their own (§6.1).""" if not path: return None segment = path.rstrip("/").rsplit("/", 1)[-1] return segment or None def _describe_element(element: IdentifierType) -&gt; dict[str, Any]: return { "id": element.get_id, "type": element.get_type, "site": element.get_sitename, "path": element.get_path, "name": name_from_path(element.get_path), } def format_search_results(elements: ListElements, *, limit: int) -&gt; dict[str, Any]: """Cascade's search endpoint has no limit/page-size param (SearchInformation has no such field) and no total-match-count field either, so limiting and counting both happen client-side here: - total_count = number of elements Cascade actually returned in THIS response (NOT a true site-wide match count - Cascade doesn't expose one). - has_more = whether client-side truncation to `limit` actually dropped any. """ identifiers = [e for e in elements.flat if isinstance(e, IdentifierType)] total_returned = len(identifiers) truncated = identifiers[:limit] return { "results": [_describe_element(e) for e in truncated], "total_count": total_returned, "has_more": total_returned &gt; limit, } ``` The `isinstance(e, IdentifierType)` filter in `format_search_results` is a defensive measure: `ListElements.elements` is typed as `list[IdentifierType | Message | Audit]` because the same container model backs `listMessages`/`readAudits` too — for `search`/`listSites` specifically it should always be `IdentifierType`, but filtering rather than crashing on a `Message`/`Audit` costs nothing and avoids a surprise `AttributeError` if Cascade's shape ever differs. --- ## 5. `server.py` ```python """FastMCP (v2 API: MCPServer) instance and tool registration for cascade-cms-rest-mcp. Phase 1: cascade_search, cascade_read_asset. """ from __future__ import annotations from typing import Any, Literal from cascade_cms.cmstypes import Asset, CascadeError, IdentifierType, ListElements, Path as CascadePath, SearchInformation from cascade_cms.wrapper import CascadeWrapperBase from mcp.server.mcpserver import MCPServer from mcp.server.mcpserver.exceptions import ToolError from . import errors, formatting from .config import cache_configuration, load_environment_variables mcp = MCPServer( name="cascade-cms", instructions=( "Read-only inspection of a Hannon Hill Cascade CMS server. Call " "cascade_search first if you don't already know an asset's id/type/path; " "then cascade_read_asset to read it (format=\"concise\" by default)." ), ) def _wrapper() -&gt; CascadeWrapperBase: """A fresh CascadeWrapperBase per tool call - see rationale below.""" return CascadeWrapperBase(load_environment_variables(), cache_configuration()) @mcp.tool() def cascade_search( query: str, site: str, asset_types: list[str] | None = None, limit: int = 20, ) -&gt; dict[str, Any]: """Search a Cascade site for assets by text; returns id/type/path/name per match. total_count/has_more describe what Cascade returned in THIS response, not a true site-wide match count (Cascade's search API exposes no such count). """ try: payload = SearchInformation( siteName=site, searchTerms=query, searchTypes=asset_types or [""], ) with _wrapper() as cascade: cascade.operations.search(payload) result = errors.single_result(cascade.submit_requests(), context="cascade_search") if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception): raise errors.search_failure_error(result) assert isinstance(result, ListElements) return formatting.format_search_results(result, limit=limit) except ToolError: raise except Exception as exc: # noqa: BLE001 - last-resort translation, see errors.py raise errors.unexpected_failure_error("cascade_search", exc) from exc @mcp.tool() def cascade_read_asset( identifier: IdentifierType | CascadePath, format: Literal["concise", "detailed"] = "concise", ) -&gt; dict[str, Any]: """Read a single Cascade asset by id+type, or by site+path+type. format="concise" (default): large/nested fields come back collapsed with an expand_with hint. format="detailed": the raw asset payload, unmodified. """ try: with _wrapper() as cascade: cascade.operations.read(identifier) result = errors.single_result(cascade.submit_requests(), context="cascade_read_asset") if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception): raise errors.read_asset_error(identifier, result) assert isinstance(result, Asset) return formatting.format_asset(result, format=format) except ToolError: raise except Exception as exc: # noqa: BLE001 - last-resort translation, see errors.py raise errors.unexpected_failure_error("cascade_read_asset", exc) from exc def main() -&gt; None: load_environment_variables() # fail fast, before starting the server mcp.run() # defaults to transport="stdio" if __name__ == "__main__": main() ``` ### Wrapper/session lifecycle decision: one `CascadeWrapperBase` per tool call **Decision: construct a fresh `CascadeWrapperBase` inside each tool call (`with _wrapper() as cascade:`), not a longer-lived shared instance.** Justification, from what's actually in the code (not speculation): - `CascadeCMSRestDriver` creates and owns a **private `asyncio` event loop per instance** (`driver.py:202-203`), driven via `run_until_complete` — not thread-safe, not reentrant. `MCPServer` dispatches sync tool functions onto worker threads (finding #2 above), and *can* run multiple tool calls concurrently on different threads. Sharing one driver/loop across those threads would be unsafe — only the thread that created an asyncio loop may drive it. - `Operations._chains` is cleared by every `submit_requests()` call regardless of outcome (`wrapper.py`, `finally: self.operations._reset_chains()`). A shared `Operations` instance across concurrent tool calls risks one call's `submit_requests()` wiping out another call's not-yet-submitted chain — a real race, not a theoretical one. - This matches **every existing precedent in the codebase** — README, all 5 skill templates, and `edit_test.py` all use a short-lived `with CascadeWrapperBase(...) as cascade:` block per script run. There is no existing pattern anywhere for a long-lived shared instance to diverge from. - Cost: a fresh `aiohttp.ClientSession` + a fresh SQLite cache-backend connection + a fresh event loop per call. All three are cheap (microseconds to low-milliseconds) and the cache itself is file-backed at a stable path (§2.2), so cached GET responses persist *across* calls even though the connection object doesn't — the caching benefit isn't lost, just the connection pooling between calls. For Phase 1's usage pattern (one local, low-QPS, single-user stdio session), this is the right tradeoff. If profiling later shows this is a bottleneck, a lock-guarded shared wrapper is a reasonable follow-up — deliberately deferred, not needed here. ### `try/except ToolError: raise; except Exception: translate` at each tool body This is the explicit safety net satisfying "never raised as bare exceptions." It also covers finding #4 (`errors.single_result` already turns an empty `submit_requests()` result into a `ToolError`, but this outer catch is what guarantees *any* other unforeseen exception — a bad cache path, a pydantic validation error building `SearchInformation`, etc.) also becomes a `ToolError` rather than a raw traceback. This mirrors the codebase's existing `except Exception as e: # noqa: BLE001 - ...` boundary convention already used in `wrapper.py`/`driver.py`. --- ## 6. Packaging `pyproject.toml` additions (note the corrected version pin vs. the locked doc's `mcp&gt;=1.0.0` — see finding #1): ```toml [project.optional-dependencies] dev = ["pytest", "pytest-asyncio", "mypy", "ruff", "build", "twine&gt;=7.0.0"] mcp = ["mcp&gt;=2.1.0,&lt;3"] [project.scripts] cascade-cms-rest-mcp = "cascade_cms.mcp.server:main" ``` And add `tests/mcp/smoke_test.py` to the existing `[tool.ruff] exclude` list, for the same reason `tests/edit_test.py` is there (manual human-run script against a live server, not production code): ```toml exclude = [ "skill/cascade-script-writer", "skill/cascade-script-writer-lite", "tests/edit_test.py", "tests/testing.py", "tests/mcp/smoke_test.py", ] ``` Corrected client-config invocation (fixes `CASCADE_API_URL` → `CASCADE_URL`, for whenever this gets documented in the README — README itself is Phase 4 per the locked doc's own phase order, so not written this round, but the correct JSON is worth locking in now): ```json { "mcpServers": { "cascade-cms": { "command": "uvx", "args": ["--from", "cascade-cms-rest[mcp]", "cascade-cms-rest-mcp"], "env": { "CASCADE_URL": "...", "CASCADE_API_KEY": "..." } } } } ``` **Guardrail**: do not add `from . import mcp` (or any mcp import) to `src/cascade_cms/__init__.py`. Confirmed by reading it that it currently only imports `driver`/`operation_logger`/`operations`/`wrapper` — this is exactly what keeps a base `pip install cascade-cms-rest` free of the new `mcp` dependency. Keep it that way. Running `tests/mcp/`'s pytest-collected tests (not the smoke test) requires both extras: `pip install -e ".[mcp,dev]"` — since `errors.py`/`server.py` import the real `mcp` package. Worth a one-line comment in `tests/mcp/conftest.py` so this isn't a surprise. --- ## 7. Tests ### 7.1 `tests/mcp/conftest.py` ```python # tests/mcp/*.py auto-collects fine under the root tests/conftest.py's # sys.path setup. smoke_test.py matches pytest's `*_test.py` glob (like # tests/edit_test.py does) but is a manual human-run script against a real # dev site, not a pytest test - exclude it the same way tests/conftest.py # excludes edit_test.py/testing.py. collect_ignore = ["smoke_test.py"] ``` ### 7.2 `tests/mcp/test_formatting.py` (pytest-collected, no live server) Covers, using plain dict fixtures (no `Asset({"asset": {...}})` wrapper needed for most cases, though a couple of `Asset(...)` cases are worth including since `format_asset` reads `asset._data`): - Scalars pass through unchanged (`str`, `int`, `float`, `bool`, `None`). - A `dict` value collapses to `{"_collapsed": True, "count": len(...), "expand_with": ...}`. - A `list` value collapses the same way, `count` = list length. - Key `"structuredData"` routes to `"cascade_get_data_structure"`. - Key `"pageConfigurations"` routes to `"cascade_get_page_config"`. - Any other dict/list key (e.g. `"metadata"`) routes to `'cascade_read_asset(format="detailed")'`. - `format="detailed"` returns `asset._data` verbatim (including nested structures, uncollapsed). - `name_from_path`: normal path (`"/a/b/c"` → `"c"`), trailing slash (`"/a/b/c/"` → `"c"`), root (`"/"` → `None`), empty string / `None` → `None`. - `format_search_results`: fewer results than `limit` → `has_more=False`, `total_count` = actual count; more than `limit` → truncated to `limit`, `has_more=True`; a non-`IdentifierType` element in `.flat` is silently filtered rather than crashing. ### 7.3 `tests/mcp/test_errors.py` (pytest-collected, no live server) - `single_result([])` raises `ToolError` mentioning connectivity/credentials. - `single_result([x])` returns `x`. - `search_failure_error(CascadeError(message="..."))` → `ToolError` whose text contains the passed-through message. - `search_failure_error(CascadeError(message=""))` → `ToolError` with the fallback text (never an empty/bare message). - `search_failure_error(SomeException("boom"))` → `ToolError` mentioning connectivity/credentials, not a bare "boom". - `read_asset_error(identifier, CascadeError(...))` → `ToolError` containing both the identifier description and a `cascade_search` suggestion. - `unexpected_failure_error` wraps an arbitrary exception with the tool name in the message. ### 7.4 `tests/mcp/test_config.py` (pytest-collected, no live server) Using `monkeypatch.setenv`/`delenv`: - Both `CASCADE_API_KEY` and `CASCADE_URL` set, `SERVER` unset → returns dict with `SERVER="default"`. - `SERVER` explicitly set → passed through. - Either or both of `CASCADE_API_KEY`/`CASCADE_URL` missing → `SystemExit` whose message names exactly the missing var name(s). - `cache_configuration()` respects `CASCADE_MCP_CACHE_DIR` override and creates the directory (use `tmp_path` from pytest, not a real `~/.cache` write). ### 7.5 `tests/mcp/test_server.py` (pytest-collected, mocked driver — stretch goal but recommended) Enabled directly by finding #3 (`@mcp.tool()` returns the plain function): `cascade_search`/`cascade_read_asset` can be called as ordinary Python functions in a test, no MCP protocol harness needed. Style mirrors `test_operation_chains.py`'s `mock_driver`/`MagicMock(spec=...)` fixtures: ```python from unittest.mock import MagicMock import pytest from cascade_cms.mcp import server class _FakeWrapper: def __init__(self, submit_result): self.operations = MagicMock() self._submit_result = submit_result def __enter__(self): return self def __exit__(self, *a): return False def submit_requests(self, *a, **k): return self._submit_result @pytest.fixture def patch_wrapper(monkeypatch): def _patch(submit_result): monkeypatch.setattr(server, "_wrapper", lambda: _FakeWrapper(submit_result)) return _patch ``` Then assert: a `ListElements` result flows through to the expected `cascade_search` dict shape; a `CascadeError` result raises `ToolError`; an `Asset` result flows through `cascade_read_asset` correctly for both `format` values; a `CascadeError`/empty-list result raises `ToolError` (never a bare exception) for `cascade_read_asset` too. This needs `CASCADE_API_KEY`/`CASCADE_URL` set to *dummy* values via `monkeypatch.setenv` since `_wrapper` is fully replaced — no real network/driver construction happens. ### 7.6 `tests/mcp/smoke_test.py` (manual, mirrors `edit_test.py`'s pattern exactly) ```python """ Title: Manual human test - MCP Phase 1 smoke test (cascade_search, cascade_read_asset) Date: &lt;fill in&gt; Description: Run directly (`python tests/mcp/smoke_test.py`), not via pytest, against a real dev Cascade site, to sanity-check cascade_search and cascade_read_asset end-to-end before wiring the server into an actual MCP client. Edit the constants below with real dev-site values first. Note: cascade_search's results will carry an expand_with hint pointing at cascade_get_data_structure/cascade_get_page_config on any data-bound asset - those tools don't exist until Phase 2. That's expected; this script only exercises the two Phase 1 tools. Expected Output: - cascade_search returns at least one result for the given query/site. - cascade_read_asset(format="concise") never contains a raw (uncollapsed) dict/list value. - cascade_read_asset(format="detailed") returns the full raw payload. Actual Output: """ import os os.environ.setdefault("CASCADE_API_KEY", "...") os.environ.setdefault("CASCADE_URL", "https://[redacted: internal-host]:8443") os.environ.setdefault("SERVER", "APPTEST") from cascade_cms.mcp.server import cascade_read_asset, cascade_search SITE = "..." QUERY = "..." results = cascade_search(query=QUERY, site=SITE) print(results) if results["results"]: first = results["results"][0] identifier = {"id": first["id"], "type": first["type"]} concise = cascade_read_asset(identifier=identifier, format="concise") print(concise) detailed = cascade_read_asset(identifier=identifier, format="detailed") print(detailed) ``` Optional complementary check worth mentioning to you (not code to write, just a note for the smoke-test step): the `mcp` SDK ships a dev inspector — `uv run mcp dev src/cascade_cms/mcp/server.py` (per the SDK's own README) — which gives a true protocol-level check (real stdio handshake, tool schema as the client would see it) as a manual, interactive complement to `smoke_test.py`'s direct-function-call check. --- ## 8. Sonnet 5 · Auto · Plan · 2026-09-02 06:39 UTC
This is genuinely valuable new information — it reveals a resolution chain the locked doc's §7 explicitly deferred as "its own dedicated conversation": a page's `contentTypeId` → a `contentType` asset → `dataDefinitionId` / `pageConfigurationSetId` / `metadataSetId`, with `contentTypePageConfigurations` mapping config names straight to IDs. And `raw_data_def.json` shows the data definition's actual schema lives as raw XML text under `xml`, not as a structured JSON tree — which is bigger than what `Asset.get_data_structure`'s group/leaf-node walk operates on (that walk expects a JSON tree of `type: "group"` nodes, which is presumably what's on a *populated* page instance, not on the definition itself). This changes the shape of Phase 2 meaningfully from what was originally scoped (plain instance-sampling via `Asset.get_data_structure`), so before I design/implement anything I want to check direction with you rather than assume.

# Phase 2 Implementation Plan: `cascade_get_data_structure` + `cascade_get_page_config`

## Design summary

The locked doc's original §6.3/§6.4 ("instance-observed sampling") is explicitly superseded. Phase 2 now reads the asset's **bound schema assets** (`contenttype` → `datadefinition`) rather than sampling the one instance queried, per the three example payloads. I validated the riskiest piece — the `iterparse`-based XML tree builder — directly against the real `raw_data_def.json` XML and it works correctly (root tag, nested groups, 8 total groups, correct `text`/`asset`/`shared-field`/`group` tag distinction, all confirmed by direct execution above).

Two mechanisms stay deliberately separate, because they answer different questions:
- **`references.py`** — a generic, type-agnostic *annotation* mechanism (`find_references`) that flags any `&lt;prefix&gt;Id` + `&lt;prefix&gt;Path`/`&lt;prefix&gt;Name` pair on **any** asset's top-level fields, for **display** in `cascade_read_asset`'s concise output. This is a discovery aid for the agent, not a resolution guarantee.
- **`resolution.py`** — the two tools' actual multi-hop I/O chains (`contentTypeId` → `contenttype` asset → `dataDefinitionId` → `datadefinition` asset), using the **specific, confirmed field names** directly (`asset._data.get("contentTypeId")`), not routed through the generic detector. Reasoning: `find_references` requires a Path/Name sibling to register a match (correct for "is this worth showing the agent"), but resolution correctness shouldn't depend on that heuristic — e.g. resolution should still succeed even if a hypothetical asset had `contentTypeId` without `contentTypePath`. Conflating the two would make tool correctness depend on a display heuristic.

## New files under `src/cascade_cms/mcp/`

### 1. `references.py` (new, pure, no I/O)

```python
_KNOWN_ASSET_TYPES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(get_args(AssetTypes.__value__))
# confirmed at runtime: 57 literal values, e.g. "contenttype", "datadefinition",
# "pageconfigurationset", "metadataset", "folder", "site" all present.

_REFERENCE_TYPE_ALIASES: dict[str, str] = {"parentfolder": "folder"}
# "parentcontainer" deliberately NOT aliased - ambiguous, mirrors Asset's
# "only what's confirmed" discipline (guessed asset_type stays None).

def guess_asset_type(prefix: str) -&gt; str | None: ...
def find_references(data: dict[str, Any]) -&gt; dict[str, dict[str, Any]]: ...
```

`find_references` scans `data`'s **top-level keys only** (mirrors `format_asset`'s existing top-level-only contract) for `key.endswith("Id")` (excluding the asset's own lowercase `"id"`, which never matches since it doesn't end in capital `"Id"`), computes `prefix = key[:-2]`, and registers an entry keyed by `prefix` if `prefix+"Path"` or `prefix+"Name"` is also present:
```python
{"id": value, "path": data.get(prefix+"Path"), "name": data.get(prefix+"Name"), "asset_type": guess_asset_type(prefix)}
```

Validated against the literal payloads:
- `typical_page_dict.json` → `{"contentType": {"id": "...", "path": "Standard Page", "name": None, "asset_type": "contenttype"}}`
- `contentType_resp.json` → 5 entries: `pageConfigurationSet`→`"pageconfigurationset"`, `dataDefinition`→`"datadefinition"`, `metadataSet`→`"metadataset"`, `parentContainer`→`asset_type: None` (ambiguous, correctly unguessed), `site` (from `siteId`+`siteName`)→`"site"`.

### 2. `data_structure.py` (new, pure, no I/O)

```python
def build_tree(xml_text: str) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:
    """iterparse(events=("start","end")) + explicit stack; node = {tag, attributes, text, children}.
    Text is captured on "end" (not "start") - elem.text isn't reliably populated
    until the closing tag is reached. elem.clear() after reading text, per the
    standard memory-bounded iterparse idiom - safe because attributes/children
    are already copied into our own dicts by then."""

def find_group(tree: dict, group_identifier: str) -&gt; dict | None:
    """Recursive descent: tag == "group" and attributes["identifier"] == group_identifier."""

def find_node(group_node: dict, node_identifier: str) -&gt; dict | None:
    """DFS within a matched group's children; descends into nested `group`
    children (never itself a match - node_identifier names a field), matches
    the first non-group child with attributes["identifier"] == node_identifier."""

def list_children(group_node: dict) -&gt; list[dict]:
    """[{"tag", "identifier", "label", "type"} for each immediate child] -
    a directory listing when node_identifier is omitted."""

def collect_group_identifiers(tree: dict) -&gt; list[str]:
    """All group identifiers anywhere in the tree - schema-authoritative
    replacement for the original doc's instance-sampled group-listing."""
```

I ran this exact logic against the real `raw_data_def.json` XML: `collect_group_identifiers` → `['page-content', 'left-column', 'right-column', 'post_details', 'homePageOptions', 'content', 'hero', 'article']` (8 groups); `find_group(tree, "right-column")` correctly locates the nested group with `['text', 'asset']` children; `post_details`'s `list_children` correctly distinguishes `text` (with `type` = `radiobutton`/`calendar`/`dropdown`/`None`), `shared-field`, `asset`, and nested `group` tags.

### 3. `resolution.py` (new, does I/O via a passed-in `cascade`)

```python
def _read(cascade: CascadeWrapperBase, asset_id: str, asset_type: str, *, context: str, purpose: str) -&gt; Asset:
    identifier = IdentifierType(identifier=asset_id, asset_type=asset_type)  # pydantic coerces bare-hex str -&gt; UUID directly, confirmed
    cascade.operations.read(identifier)
    result = errors.single_result(cascade.submit_requests(), context=context)
    if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception):
        raise errors.read_asset_error(identifier, result, context=context, purpose=purpose)
    assert isinstance(result, Asset)
    return result

def resolve_content_type(asset: Asset, cascade: CascadeWrapperBase, *, context: str) -&gt; Asset:
    content_type_id = asset._data.get("contentTypeId")
    if not content_type_id:
        raise errors.no_resolvable_reference_error(asset, ["contentTypeId"], context=context)
    return _read(cascade, content_type_id, "contenttype", context=context, purpose="the content type")

def resolve_data_definition(asset: Asset, cascade: CascadeWrapperBase, *, context: str) -&gt; Asset:
    # (a) direct field - NOT confirmed by any fixture seen; harmless no-op if absent.
    data_definition_id = asset._data.get("dataDefinitionId")
    if data_definition_id:
        return _read(cascade, data_definition_id, "datadefinition", context=context, purpose="the data definition")
    # (b) via contentTypeId -&gt; contentType.dataDefinitionId (confirmed by contentType_resp.json)
    content_type = resolve_content_type(asset, cascade, context=context)
    data_definition_id = content_type._data.get("dataDefinitionId")
    if not data_definition_id:
        raise errors.no_resolvable_reference_error(content_type, ["dataDefinitionId"], context=context)
    return _read(cascade, data_definition_id, "datadefinition", context=context, purpose="the data definition")
```

Both hops reuse the **same `cascade`** passed in from server.py's one `with _wrapper() as cascade:` block, calling `submit_requests()` sequentially per the established reuse pattern.

## Changed files

### `errors.py` — extend, don't replace

- **`read_asset_error(identifier, result, *, context="cascade_read_asset", purpose=None)`** — add two optional kwargs with defaults that preserve the existing call site and existing test (`test_read_asset_error_mentions_identifier_and_suggests_search`) byte-for-byte. New call sites pass `context="cascade_get_data_structure"` / `context="cascade_get_page_config"` and `purpose="the content type"` / `"the data definition"`.
- **`no_resolvable_reference_error(asset: Asset, tried_fields: list[str], *, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`** — names the fields tried and lists the fields actually present (`sorted(asset._data.keys())`), suggesting `cascade_read_asset(format="detailed")`.
- **`no_xml_field_error(data_definition: Asset, *, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`**
- **`group_not_found_error(data_definition: Asset, available_groups: list[str], group: str, *, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`** — `available_groups` computed by the caller via `data_structure.collect_group_identifiers(tree)` (keeps `errors.py` free of any XML-tree-shape knowledge — plain `list[str]` in).
- **`node_not_found_error(data_definition: Asset, group: str, available_identifiers: list[str], node_identifier: str, *, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`**
- **`config_name_not_found_error(content_type: Asset, available_names: list[str], configuration_name: str, *, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`**
- **`page_region_not_found_error(asset: Asset, configuration_name: str, page_region: str, available_regions: list[str], *, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`** — message explicitly notes this can mean "schema-valid configuration name, but this particular instance was never authored with content for this region" (documents the genuine instance-data limitation).
- **`page_region_requires_configuration_name_error(*, context: str) -&gt; ToolError`** — cheap param-validation error, no I/O.

### `formatting.py`

```python
from .references import find_references

def format_asset(asset: Asset, *, format: Literal["concise", "detailed"]) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:
    if format == "detailed":
        return dict(asset._data)
    collapsed = {key: _collapse_value(key, value) for key, value in asset._data.items()}
    references = find_references(asset._data)
    if references:
        collapsed["_references"] = references
    return collapsed
```

`_references` is **additive only** — omitted entirely when empty, so the existing `test_format_asset_concise_never_leaves_uncollapsed_dict_or_list` fixture (no `Id`/`Path` pairs) still passes unmodified. Add a module comment noting `_references` is a **deliberate exception** to the collapse rule (synthesized, not passed through from `_data`).

### `server.py`

Two new `@mcp.tool()` handlers, each doing exactly one `with _wrapper() as cascade:` block covering every read hop, following the existing `try/except ToolError: raise/except Exception: unexpected_failure_error` pattern:

```python
@mcp.tool()
def cascade_get_data_structure(
    identifier: IdentifierType | CascadePath,
    group: str,
    node_identifier: str | None = None,
) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:
    try:
        with _wrapper() as cascade:
            cascade.operations.read(identifier)
            result = errors.single_result(cascade.submit_requests(), context="cascade_get_data_structure")
            if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception):
                raise errors.read_asset_error(identifier, result)
            assert isinstance(result, Asset)
            data_definition = resolution.resolve_data_definition(result, cascade, context="cascade_get_data_structure")

        xml_text = data_definition._data.get("xml")
        if not xml_text:
            raise errors.no_xml_field_error(data_definition, context="cascade_get_data_structure")
        tree = data_structure.build_tree(xml_text)

        group_node = data_structure.find_group(tree, group)
        if group_node is None:
            raise errors.group_not_found_error(
                data_definition, data_structure.collect_group_identifiers(tree), group, context="cascade_get_data_structure"
            )

        meta = {"id": data_definition.get("id"), "path": data_definition.get("path")}
        if node_identifier is None:
            return {"group": group, "data_definition": meta, "children": data_structure.list_children(group_node)}

        node = data_structure.find_node(group_node, node_identifier)
        if node is None:
            available = [c["identifier"] for c in data_structure.list_children(group_node) if c["identifier"]]
            raise errors.node_not_found_error(data_definition, group, available, node_identifier, context="cascade_get_data_structure")
        return {"group": group, "data_definition": meta, "node": node}
    except ToolError:
        raise
    except Exception as exc:
        raise errors.unexpected_failure_error("cascade_get_data_structure", exc) from exc


@mcp.tool()
def cascade_get_page_config(
    identifier: IdentifierType | CascadePath,
    configuration_name: str | None = None,
    page_region: str | None = None,
) -&gt; dict[str, Any]:
    try:
        if page_region is not None and configuration_name is None:
            raise errors.page_region_requires_configuration_name_error(context="cascade_get_page_config")

        with _wrapper() as cascade:
            cascade.operations.read(identifier)
            result = errors.single_result(cascade.submit_requests(), context="cascade_get_page_config")
            if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception):
                raise errors.read_asset_error(identifier, result)
            assert isinstance(result, Asset)
            asset = result
            content_type = resolution.resolve_content_type(asset, cascade, context="cascade_get_page_config")

        configs = content_type._data.get("contentTypePageConfigurations") or []
        available = [
            {"name": c.get("pageConfigurationName"), "id": c.get("pageConfigurationId"), "publish_mode": c.get("publishMode")}
            for c in configs
        ]

        if configuration_name is None:
            return {"content_type": content_type.get("name"), "configurations": available}

        match = next((c for c in available if c["name"] == configuration_name), None)
        if match is None:
            names = [c["name"] for c in available]
            raise errors.config_name_not_found_error(content_type, names, configuration_name, context="cascade_get_page_config")

        instance_config = next((c for c in asset._page_configs if c.name == configuration_name), None)
        region_names = [r.name for r in instance_config.pageRegions] if instance_config else []

        if page_region is None:
            return {"configuration": match, "region_names_on_this_instance": region_names}

        region = asset.get_page_configuration(configuration_name, page_region)
        if region is None:
            raise errors.page_region_not_found_error(asset, configuration_name, page_region, region_names, context="cascade_get_page_config")
        return {"configuration": match, "region": region.model_dump()}
    except ToolError:
        raise
    except Exception as exc:
        raise errors.unexpected_failure_error("cascade_get_page_config", exc) from exc
```

Also: add `from . import data_structure, resolution` to imports; update the `mcp = MCPServer(instructions=...)` string to mention the two new tools; update the module docstring header ("Phase 1: ... Phase 2: cascade_get_data_structure, cascade_get_page_config").

Key split for `cascade_get_page_config`: **names/publish-mode are schema** (from `content_type._data["contentTypePageConfigurations"]`, confirmed shape: `pageConfigurationId`/`pageConfigurationName`/`publishMode`, nothing about regions), **region content is instance data** (from `asset.get_page_configuration`/`asset._page_configs`, the existing tested library method — reused, not discarded). This split is directly grounded in the payloads: `contentType_resp.json` genuinely has no region/content field at all, so region content *must* come from the target asset's own `pageConfigurations`.

## Unconfirmed assumptions to flag explicitly (not silently guessed)

1. **Direct `dataDefinitionId` on a non-page asset** (e.g. a block) — no fixture confirms this field name/shape. Implemented as a harmless short-circuit check only; if absent, falls through to the confirmed `contentTypeId` chain. Flag for smoke-test confirmation.
2. **`pageconfigurationset`/`pageconfiguration` asset payload shape** — no raw example exists. Deliberately **not read or modeled** anywhere in this plan; only `contentType.contentTypePageConfigurations` (which *is* confirmed) is used.
3. **Whether a data definition reached via a direct `dataDefinitionId` is always `asset_type="datadefinition"` vs. sometimes `"structureddatadefinition"`** — no fixture distinguishes these; if wrong, Cascade will return a clear not-found/type error surfaced verbatim via `read_asset_error`.
4. **`AssetTypes.__value__` runtime introspection** (`get_args(AssetTypes.__value__)` to get the flat 57-value literal set) — verified working in this exact venv/Python 3.14, but is a slightly unusual reliance on PEP 695 `TypeAliasType` internals rather than an existing repo precedent. Add a guard test asserting expected literals are present, so any future typing-internals change fails loudly rather than silently corrupting `guess_asset_type`.

.venv/bin/python -c "
import json, io
import xml.etree.ElementTree as ET

with open('raw_data_def.json') as f:
    data = json.load(f)
xml_text = data['asset']['dataDefinition']['xml']

def build_tree(xml_text):
    stack = []
    root = None
    for event, elem in ET.iterparse(io.StringIO(xml_text), events=('start', 'end')):
        if event == 'start':
            node = {'tag': elem.tag}
            if elem.attrib:
                node['attributes'] = dict(elem.attrib)
            stack.append((node, []))
        else:
            node, children = stack.pop()
            if children:
                node['children'] = children
            if stack:
                stack[-1][1].append(node)
            else:
                root = node
            elem.clear()
    return root

tree = build_tree(xml_text)
print('root tag:', tree['tag'])

def collect_groups(node, acc):
    if node.get('tag') == 'group':
        acc.append(node['attributes']['identifier'])
    for c in node.get('children', []):
        collect_groups(c, acc)
    return acc

groups = collect_groups(tree, [])
print('groups:', groups)
print('count:', len(groups))

{"plan": "# Phase 2 Implementation Plan — Schema-Authoritative Discovery\n\n## Context\n\nPhase 1 (`cascade_search`, `cascade_read_asset`) is implemented, tested, and merged\ninto `src/cascade_cms/mcp/`. The locked design doc originally scoped Phase 2\n(`cascade_get_data_structure`, `cascade_get_page_config`) as si...", "planFilePath": "/home/Coder/.claude/plans/create-a-plan-for-parallel-hejlsberg.md"}
Good question — worth being precise about, since "token optimization" and the `iterparse` choice are actually solving two different problems.

**Token cost (what crosses into the LLM's context) is addressed by the tool's parameter shape, not by the parser.** `cascade_get_data_structure` requires `group` and makes `node_identifier` optional — so a call never returns the whole data definition:
- `node_identifier` omitted → `list_children(group_node)` returns only that group's **immediate** children (tag/identifier/label/type per child, one level deep) — nested subgroups show up as a single summary line each, not expanded. Drilling into `main-content/imageGrid`, say, is a *separate* call with `group="imageGrid"`.
- `node_identifier` given → returns exactly one leaf field's definition.

This mirrors the same collapse-by-default/expand-on-demand pattern already established in Phase 1's `cascade_read_asset` (concise vs. detailed) — the agent controls slice size per call, and a 38KB document like `data-def.xml` (~35 groups deep) is never dumped in one shot.

**One place this could still grow**: `group_not_found_error`'s "available groups" listing uses `collect_group_identifiers()`, which is flat across the *whole* tree regardless of depth — for `data-def.xml` that's ~35 names, fine as a self-correcting error menu, but a much larger data definition (hundreds of groups) could make that list itself non-trivial. I'll note this in the plan as a flagged consideration rather than pre-emptively adding truncation logic I can't validate against a real example that large.

**`iterparse` is solving a different problem — CPU/memory during parsing, not token cost.** Streaming with `elem.clear()` on each "end" event discards each element's C-backing incrementally, bounding peak memory below a full `ET.fromstring()` DOM build. But the parser still returns one complete Python dict tree — the token savings come entirely from only *returning a slice of it* (§ above), not from the parsing strategy itself. Also worth noting: `build_tree()` re-parses the raw XML fresh on every call (the HTTP fetch of the `xml` field is cached by the existing SQLite response cache from Phase 1, but the parse isn't memoized) — that's a repeated-CPU tradeoff for the token-slicing benefit, and given `iterparse` over 38KB is low-millisecond work, not worth optimizing further right now.

I'll add this reasoning to the plan file as explicit rationale rather than leaving it implicit.

Sonnet 5 · Plan · 2026-09-02 07:28 UTC
No — it's **truncation with disclosure**, not pagination. `truncate_list`/`format_names_for_message` show only the first `limit` items and tell the caller how many more exist (`total_count`/`has_more`, or "and N more" in error text), but there's no mechanism to actually retrieve the remainder — no `offset`/`cursor` param, no "next page" call. This exactly matches what `cascade_search`'s `limit` param already does in Phase 1 (which also has no offset — partly because Cascade's search endpoint itself exposes no server-side paging).

The difference for Phase 2's listings: unlike search, these lists (`list_children`, `collect_group_identifiers`, the config list) are fully computed server-side from data we already hold in memory (the parsed XML tree, or the contentType asset), so real pagination *would* be cheap to add here — nothing upstream blocks it the way Cascade's search API blocks it for `cascade_search`.

I didn't design real pagination because the tools already have a narrower, better escape hatch: instead of paging through a long list of groups, the agent just calls again with a specific `group=`/`configuration_name=` to go straight to the one it wants. Paging makes sense when you need to *enumerate everything*; here the expected usage is "find the one field/config I need," so truncation-with-disclosure (plus narrowing) covers it without adding `offset`/`cursor` params to every tool signature.

Do you want real pagination instead (an `offset` or `cursor` param on the listing paths), or does truncate-and-disclose-plus-narrow cover the case you're thinking of?


Keith · 2026-09-02 07:29 UTC
Could you list potential pros and cons for each?


Sonnet 5 · Plan · 2026-09-02 07:30 UTC
Here's the comparison, grounded in what these specific tools actually need:

**Approach A — Truncate-and-disclose (current plan: `total_count`/`has_more`, no way to fetch the rest)**

Pros:
- Zero new parameter surface — `cascade_get_data_structure`/`cascade_get_page_config` keep their current signatures; nothing new for the agent to learn or misuse.
- Matches the precedent already shipped in Phase 1 (`cascade_search`'s `limit`) — one consistent mental model across the whole server.
- No pagination state to manage — no cursor generation/validation/expiry, no off-by-one risk.
- The tools' actual use case is "find this one specific field/config," not "enumerate everything" — narrowing (`group=`, `configuration_name=`) is a more direct path to that than paging through results blindly.
- A real fallback already exists for the rare "I need literally everything" case: `cascade_read_asset(identifier=<dataDefinition>, format="detailed")` returns the complete raw `xml` string untouched (Phase 1's detailed mode is a verbatim `_data` dump) — an agent that truly needs the full schema can already get it and parse it itself, bypassing the cap entirely.

Cons:
- If an agent genuinely needs a complete enumeration (e.g., generating a script template that must cover every field), there's no way to walk past item 50 through these tools — only the detailed-mode fallback above, which hands back unparsed XML.
- The first 50 items shown are in arbitrary tree-walk order — nothing guarantees the "relevant" one is visible before the cut, so a truncated listing could quietly hide the very item an agent was browsing for, with no signal beyond "has_more: true" to prompt it to narrow further.

**Approach B — Real pagination (add an `offset` param to the listing paths)**

Pros:
- Deterministic, complete enumeration is possible — matters for bulk/audit-style use (e.g., "cover every field in this content type").
- No risk of a relevant item being silently hidden past the cutoff — an agent can walk to the end with confidence.
- Cheaper to implement than pagination usually is here: since `build_tree()`/the config list are re-derived fresh and deterministically on every call (no server-side session state, nothing cached between calls), a plain `offset: int = 0` suffices — no opaque cursor token, no statefulness bugs to worry about.

Cons:
- Adds a parameter to tools that already have several (`group`/`node_identifier`, `configuration_name`/`page_region`) — more surface for an agent to reach for instead of just narrowing directly, which is almost always the better move for this tool's actual use case.
- Still doesn't remove the ambiguity of "how far do I page before giving up" — just gives the agent the means to resolve it at the cost of more round trips.
- Each page is still a fresh asset-read + XML-reparse (nothing cached between calls), so paging through a hypothetically huge definition means repeating that work N/50 times — more tool-call/token overhead than one detailed-mode fallback call.
- More code and edge cases to get right (offset past the end, negative offset, etc.) for a scenario that hasn't been observed yet.

My read: given nothing seen so far comes close to the cap, the tools are meant for targeted lookup not bulk enumeration, and a genuine full-schema fallback already exists via detailed-mode `cascade_read_asset`, I'd keep truncate-and-disclose for now and treat pagination as a cheap add-later if real usage shows the cap actually biting. But you flagged wanting to plan ahead here — happy to add the `offset` param now instead if you'd rather have it built in from the start.


Keith · 2026-09-02 07:32 UTC
What are some potential fixes for the 2 cons in `Approach A`?


Sonnet 5 · Plan · 2026-09-02 07:33 UTC
**Con 1 fixes — "no way through these tools past item 50, only the raw detailed-mode fallback":**

1. **Point at the fallback explicitly, in-band.** When `has_more: true`, add an `expand_with`-style hint to the response — e.g. `"expand_with": "cascade_read_asset(identifier=<data_definition>, format=\"detailed\")"` — reusing the exact convention Phase 1 already established for collapsed fields. Costs nothing new to build, makes the existing escape hatch self-discoverable instead of something the agent has to already know about.
2. **Add an explicit override `limit` param** (e.g. `limit: int | None = None` on `cascade_get_data_structure`/`cascade_get_page_config`, `None` → default 50) so an agent that deliberately wants everything can ask for it in one call — `items[:limit]` already trivially honors a bigger number since the full list is already computed server-side. No round-trips, no cursor, much smaller change than full pagination.
3. **Raise the default cap** (e.g. 50 → 200) so truncation essentially never fires against anything realistically sized, purely reducing how often the con matters rather than solving it. Cheapest option, weakest fix.
4. **Full pagination** (Approach B from before) — the complete fix, but heavier than the other three for a con that hasn't been observed to actually bite yet.

I'd combine #1 + #2: both are small, additive, consistent with existing conventions, and together they mean an agent is never actually stuck — it's told where the rest lives, and can ask for more in one call if it wants to.

**Con 2 fixes — "arbitrary truncation order could hide the relevant item":**

1. **Sort before truncating** — alphabetically by `identifier`/`name` instead of raw XML tree-walk order. Same determinism, but now scannable: an agent (or a human glancing at output) can see "the list only goes up to 'm' " and infer their target might be past the cut, which arbitrary document order doesn't give you.
2. **Relevance-first ordering specifically on the error-path "available X" listings.** These fire because the agent already guessed a *specific* wrong identifier — that guess is a free relevance signal. Sort entries containing/close to the guessed name first, then alphabetically for the rest, before truncating. This is the more targeted fix, since it concentrates exactly where con 2 actually causes harm (a failed lookup, not a successful browse).
3. **Optional substring filter param** on the listing paths (e.g. `contains: str | None`) so an agent with a rough idea what it's looking for can narrow server-side instead of getting a blind top-50 slice. More capable, but it's new param surface again.

I'd combine #1 (cheap, applies everywhere, no new params) + #2 (targeted at the error paths, also no new params — just a smarter sort using data we already have) and skip #3 unless you want that filtering capability for its own sake, not just as a truncation fix.

Want me to fold the recommended combination (#1+#2 from each) into the plan, or adjust the mix first?


Keith · 2026-09-02 07:35 UTC
Alright I think fold those recommendations into the plan.

import json

from cascade_cms.mcp import resolution

class FakeWrapper:
    def __init__(self, results_sequence):
        self.operations = MagicMock()
        self._seq = list(results_sequence)
    def submit_requests(self, *a, **k):
        return [self._seq.pop(0)]

with open('contentType_resp.json') as f:
    content_type = Asset(json.load(f))
with open('raw_data_def.json') as f:
    data_def = Asset(json.load(f))

# Synthetic page using a real bare-hex id (the placeholder '...' in the condensed
# example isn't valid for a UUID-coercion test)
page = Asset({'asset': {'page': {'contentTypeId': content_type.get('id')}}})

direct_asset = Asset({'asset': {'block': {'dataDefinitionId': data_def.get('id')}}})

print('direct short-circuit OK')

    resolution.resolve_data_definition(no_ref_asset, FakeWrapper([]), context='test')

import json
from unittest.mock import MagicMock
from cascade_cms.cmstypes import Asset
from cascade_cms.mcp import server

class SeqWrapper:
    def __init__(self, seq):
        self.operations = MagicMock()
        self._seq = list(seq)
    def __enter__(self): return self
    def __exit__(self, *a): return False
    def submit_requests(self, *a, **k):
        return [self._seq.pop(0)]

with open('typical_page_dict.json') as f:
    page_raw = json.load(f)
with open('contentType_resp.json') as f:
    content_type = Asset(json.load(f))
with open('raw_data_def.json') as f:
