The user also explicitly generalized the requirement: "this applies to any response that contains `Id` field similar it it's appended with `Path` like `parentFolderPath`. this tool should apply universal to Cascade assets" — i.e., a generic, asset-type-agnostic reference-detection mechanism, not hardcoded to just `contentTypeId`.
   - Mid-Phase-2-design, the user asked a series of probing design questions that I was expected to answer thoroughly and then fold into the plan: token-cost implications, whether the truncation approach was "pagination," pros/cons of truncate-and-disclose vs. real pagination, and concrete fixes for the two cons of the chosen approach — culminating in "Alright I think fold those recommendations into the plan," which I did before final `ExitPlanMode` approval.
   - Explicit constraint from earlier in the session (Phase 1 planning): the user does not want anything git-committed yet ("I don't want to commit those changes to repo yet") — no commits have been made in this session; this constraint should be treated as still in effect for Phase 2's new files unless the user says otherwise.
   - No other explicit security/credential-handling constraints were stated beyond the library's existing convention of reading `CASCADE_API_KEY`/`CASCADE_URL`/`SERVER` from environment variables (never hardcoded).

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MCP (Model Context Protocol) Python SDK v2.x** — `FastMCP` renamed to `mcp.server.mcpserver.MCPServer`; `ToolError` at `mcp.server.mcpserver.exceptions.ToolError`; `@mcp.tool()` decorator returns the original function unchanged (directly testable); sync tool functions auto-offloaded to worker threads via `anyio.to_thread.run_sync`; `mcp.run()` defaults to `transport="stdio"`. Pinned as `mcp>=2.1.0,<3` in the project's `dev` optional-dependency group (not a separate extra — this was a major design pivot, see below).
   - **Distribution model pivot**: `MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_REV.md` (superseding the original `MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`) established that `mcp/` is *not* a `pip install cascade-cms-rest[mcp]` extra — the `mcp` SDK dependency lives only in the existing `dev` group (for contributors), and end-user distribution will eventually mirror the `skill/` folder's GitHub-Release-artifact pattern (build mechanics explicitly deferred to a future "Phase 4" conversation). No `[project.scripts]` console entry was added this round.
   - **`cascade_cms` library internals**: `Operations.read/search/listSites` return `OperationChain`/`ChainGroup` that resolve via `CascadeWrapperBase.submit_requests()` to typed Pydantic objects (`Asset`, `ListElements`) or `CascadeError` — failures are *values*, never raised exceptions, so every result needs an `isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception)` check. `CascadeWrapperBase(environmentVariables, configurationVariables)` wraps a `CascadeCMSRestDriver` which owns a private, non-reentrant `asyncio` event loop per instance — driving the design decision to construct a fresh `CascadeWrapperBase` per MCP tool call (not shared/pooled), reusing the *same* wrapper instance for multiple sequential reads *within* one tool call when a multi-hop resolution is needed.
   - **`Asset`** (`cmstypes.py`) is a thin dict-backed wrapper: `Asset._data` holds the raw per-type field dict by reference; `Asset.get(key)` raises `KeyError` on miss (recently changed from silent `None`, per uncommitted prep work from before this session); `Asset._page_configs: list[PageConfiguration]` is eagerly parsed from `_data["pageConfigurations"]`; `Asset.get_data_structure`/`get_page_configuration` are the pre-existing *instance-sampling* methods, still reused (not discarded) for genuinely per-instance data (page-region content).
   - **Cascade's cross-asset reference convention** (discovered from real payloads, not documented anywhere): any field ending in `Id` with a sibling `<prefix>Path` or `<prefix>Name` field is a reference to another asset — confirmed across multiple real asset types (page → `contentTypeId`/`contentTypePath`; contentType → `dataDefinitionId`/`dataDefinitionPath`, `pageConfigurationSetId`/`pageConfigurationSetPath`, `metadataSetId`/`metadataSetPath`, `parentContainerId`/`parentContainerPath`; `siteId`/`siteName`).
   - **`AssetTypes`** is a PEP 695 `type AssetTypes = Literal[...]` alias with 57 lowercase string values; introspectable at runtime via `get_args(AssetTypes.__value__)` (verified working in this exact venv/Python 3.14) — used to build `_KNOWN_ASSET_TYPES` frozenset for guessing a reference field's target asset type from its name prefix (mirroring the existing `Asset._ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS`/`_ASSET_TYPE_KEY_ALIASES` "only guess what's confirmed" discipline).
   - **Cascade `system-data-structure` XML documents** (data-definition schemas): confirmed via three real XML samples (`raw_data_def.json`'s embedded xml, plus repo-root `data-def.xml` [38KB, 43 groups] and `shared-field-exm.xml`) to use exactly 7 element tags (`system-data-structure`, `group`, `text`, `asset`, `shared-field`, `radio-item`, `dropdown-item`) with 15+ distinct, open-ended attribute names — leading to the decision to represent every element generically as `{"tag": ..., "attributes": {...}, "children": [...]}` rather than hand-modeling specific field types.
   - **`xml.etree.ElementTree.iterparse`** (stdlib, no new dependency) — used with `events=("start", "end")` and an explicit stack, calling `elem.clear()` on each "end" event (standard memory-bounded idiom); comments in the XML are never emitted by iterparse with just `("start","end")` so need no special skipping.
   - **Token-cost mitigation strategy** (extensively discussed and refined per user's explicit "better safe than sorry" request): a shared `DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT = 50` constant backs two presentation shapes — `truncate_list()` (dict-shaped, `total_count`/`has_more`, mirrors `cascade_search`'s existing pattern, used for successful listings) and `format_names_for_message()` (message-shaped, capped comma-join with "and N more", used inside `ToolError` text). Three specific mitigations were designed after an explicit pros/cons discussion of "truncate-and-disclose" vs. "real pagination" (offset/cursor) — the user chose to enrich truncate-and-disclose rather than add pagination:
     1. Deterministic alphabetical sort before truncating (`list_children`/`collect_group_identifiers`), replacing arbitrary XML-tree-walk order.
     2. Relevance-first sort (`sort_by_relevance(names, guess)`) specifically on "not found" error listings, using the agent's own failed guess as a free relevance signal.
     3. An explicit `limit: int | None = None` override param on both new tools, plus an `expand_with` hint (reusing the exact convention from `_collapse_value`'s Phase 1 collapsed-field hints) pointing at `cascade_read_asset(identifier=..., format="detailed")` on the underlying data-definition/content-type asset when truncation occurs — giving a self-correcting, already-uncapped fallback.
   - **Self-correcting `ToolError` philosophy** (established in Phase 1, extended in Phase 2): every error names what was tried, what's actually available, and/or which tool to call next — never a bare "not found" or raw traceback.
   - **Testing conventions**: pytest, no live server, hand-built `Asset(...)`/dict fixtures; `_FakeWrapper` (single pre-baked result) from Phase 1 vs. new `SequentialFakeWrapper` (one pre-baked result per sequential `submit_requests()` call, for multi-hop resolution chains) added to `tests/mcp/conftest.py` for Phase 2.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/references.py`** (new, Phase 2) — full final content:
     ```python
     """Generic, asset-type-agnostic detection of cross-asset references.
     ...
     """
     from __future__ import annotations
     from typing import Any, get_args
     from cascade_cms.cmstypes import AssetTypes

     _KNOWN_ASSET_TYPES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(get_args(AssetTypes.__value__))
     _REFERENCE_TYPE_ALIASES: dict[str, str] = {"parentfolder": "folder"}

     def guess_asset_type(prefix: str) -> str | None:
         candidate = prefix.lower()
         if candidate in _KNOWN_ASSET_TYPES:
             return candidate
         return _REFERENCE_TYPE_ALIASES.get(candidate)

     def find_references(data: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, dict[str, Any]]:
         references: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
         for key, value in data.items():
             if not key.endswith("Id"):
                 continue
             prefix = key[: -len("Id")]
             if not prefix:
                 continue
             path_key = prefix + "Path"
             name_key = prefix + "Name"
             if path_key not in data and name_key not in data:
                 continue
             references[prefix] = {
                 "id": value,
                 "path": data.get(path_key),
                 "name": data.get(name_key),
                 "asset_type": guess_asset_type(prefix),
             }
         return references
     ```
     Verified directly against `contentType_resp.json` via Bash — produced exactly the expected 5-entry dict.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/data_structure.py`** (new, Phase 2) — full final content includes `build_tree(xml_text)` (iterparse-based, stack of `(node, children_list)` tuples, `node["attributes"]`/`node["children"]` only set if non-empty), `_identifier(node)`, `find_group(tree, group_identifier)` (recursive descent), `find_node(group_node, node_identifier)` (DFS, descends into nested `group` children, matches first non-`group` tag with matching identifier), `list_children(group_node)` (returns `{tag, identifier, label, type}` dicts, **sorted alphabetically by identifier**), `collect_group_identifiers(tree)` (recursive walk, **sorted, uncapped** — truncation applied only at presentation layer). Verified against both `raw_data_def.json` (8 groups) and repo-root `data-def.xml` (43 groups total; `main-content` group has exactly 20 children) via direct Bash execution.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/resolution.py`** (new, Phase 2) — full final content:
     ```python
     from __future__ import annotations
     from typing import Any, cast
     from cascade_cms.cmstypes import Asset, AssetTypes, CascadeError, IdentifierType
     from cascade_cms.wrapper import CascadeWrapperBase
     from . import errors

     def _read(cascade, asset_id: str, asset_type: str, *, context: str, purpose: str) -> Asset:
         identifier = IdentifierType(identifier=cast(Any, asset_id), asset_type=cast(AssetTypes, asset_type))
         cascade.operations.read(identifier)
         result = errors.single_result(cascade.submit_requests(), context=context)
         if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception):
             raise errors.read_asset_error(identifier, result, context=context, purpose=purpose)
         assert isinstance(result, Asset)
         return result

     def resolve_content_type(asset: Asset, cascade, *, context: str) -> Asset:
         content_type_id = asset._data.get("contentTypeId")
         if not content_type_id:
             raise errors.no_resolvable_reference_error(asset, ["contentTypeId"], context=context)
         return _read(cascade, content_type_id, "contenttype", context=context, purpose="the content type")

     def resolve_data_definition(asset: Asset, cascade, *, context: str) -> Asset:
         data_definition_id = asset._data.get("dataDefinitionId")
         if data_definition_id:
             return _read(cascade, data_definition_id, "datadefinition", context=context, purpose="the data definition")
         content_type = resolve_content_type(asset, cascade, context=context)
         data_definition_id = content_type._data.get("dataDefinitionId")
         if not data_definition_id:
             raise errors.no_resolvable_reference_error(content_type, ["dataDefinitionId"], context=context)
         return _read(cascade, data_definition_id, "datadefinition", context=context, purpose="the data definition")
     ```
     mypy initially failed on `identifier=asset_id`/`asset_type=asset_type` (str vs UUID/Literal) — fixed via `cast(Any, ...)`/`cast(AssetTypes, ...)`. Verified end-to-end (two-hop chain, direct short-circuit, no-reference error) against real fixtures via Bash.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/errors.py`** (extended, Phase 2) — added `Asset` import and `from .formatting import format_names_for_message, sort_by_relevance`. `read_asset_error` signature changed to:
     ```python
     def read_asset_error(
         identifier: IdentifierType | Path,
         result: CascadeError | Exception,
         *,
         context: str = "cascade_read_asset",
         purpose: str | None = None,
     ) -> ToolError:
     ```
     (message now interpolates `{context}`/`{target}` instead of hardcoded "cascade_read_asset" — confirmed safe since existing tests only assert substrings). Added 7 new constructors: `no_resolvable_reference_error`, `no_xml_field_error`, `group_not_found_error`, `node_not_found_error`, `config_name_not_found_error`, `page_region_not_found_error` (notes "valid configuration but never authored" as an honest limitation), `page_region_requires_configuration_name_error` — all using `sort_by_relevance(...)` + `format_names_for_message(...) or "(none)"`.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/formatting.py`** (extended, Phase 2) — added `from .references import find_references`; `format_asset` now does:
     ```python
     def format_asset(asset: Asset, *, format: Literal["concise", "detailed"]) -> dict[str, Any]:
         if format == "detailed":
             return dict(asset._data)
         collapsed = {key: _collapse_value(key, value) for key, value in asset._data.items()}
         references = find_references(asset._data)
         if references:
             collapsed["_references"] = references
         return collapsed
     ```
     Added at end of file:
     ```python
     DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT = 50

     def truncate_list(items, *, key, limit=DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT, expand_with=None) -> dict:
         result = {key: items[:limit], "total_count": len(items), "has_more": len(items) > limit}
         if len(items) > limit and expand_with:
             result["expand_with"] = expand_with
         return result

     def sort_by_relevance(names, guess) -> list[str]:
         guess_lower = guess.lower()
         matches = sorted(n for n in names if guess_lower in n.lower())
         rest = sorted(n for n in names if guess_lower not in n.lower())
         return matches + rest

     def format_names_for_message(names, *, limit=DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT) -> str:
         shown = ", ".join(names[:limit])
         if len(names) > limit:
             shown += f", and {len(names) - limit} more"
         return shown
     ```

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/server.py`** (extended, Phase 2) — imports now include `data_structure, resolution`; module docstring and `MCPServer(instructions=...)` updated to mention both new tools. Added:
     - `cascade_get_data_structure(identifier, group, node_identifier=None, limit=None)` — reads target asset, calls `resolution.resolve_data_definition`, parses `_data.get("xml")` via `data_structure.build_tree`, `find_group`, then either `truncate_list(list_children(...), key="children", limit=..., expand_with=<cascade_read_asset(...) on the data definition>)` or `find_node(...)`; raises `no_xml_field_error`/`group_not_found_error`/`node_not_found_error` as appropriate.
     - `cascade_get_page_config(identifier, configuration_name=None, page_region=None, limit=None)` — validates `page_region` requires `configuration_name`; reads target asset, calls `resolution.resolve_content_type`; builds `available` list from `content_type._data["contentTypePageConfigurations"]` (sorted by name); when `configuration_name` given, looks up `instance_config = next((c for c in asset._page_configs if c.name == configuration_name), None)` for `region_names_on_this_instance` (instance-sourced, not content-type-sourced — deliberately tested this split); when `page_region` also given, calls the pre-existing `asset.get_page_configuration(configuration_name, page_region)` and returns `region.model_dump()`.
     Both wrapped in the same `try: ... except ToolError: raise; except Exception as exc: raise errors.unexpected_failure_error(...) from exc` pattern as Phase 1's tools.

What format does the `QUERY` string accept?


Sonnet 5 · Auto · 2026-09-02 23:09 UTC
Bashgrep -rn "def search" src/cascade_cms/ | head -20<1s
grep -rn "def search" src/cascade_cms/ | head -20
Readsrc/cascade_cms/operations.py<1s
/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/operations.py
Bashgrep -n "class SearchInformation" -A 30 src/cascade_cms/cmstypes.py<1s
grep -n "class SearchInformation" -A 30 src/cascade_cms/cmstypes.py
Bashgrep -n "def cascade_search" -A 40 src/cascade_cms/mcp/server.py | head -60<1s
grep -n "def cascade_search" -A 40 src/cascade_cms/mcp/server.py | head -60
`QUERY` isn't validated or transformed anywhere in this codebase — it's passed straight through as the `searchTerms` field of `SearchInformation` (`cmstypes.py:799-809`) to Cascade's REST `search` endpoint (`operations.py:429-438`, a POST to `search` with that payload). The library does no parsing, escaping, or wildcarding of it itself.

So the accepted format is whatever Cascade's own `search` API endpoint accepts server-side — the same free-text query the Cascade UI's global search box takes (asset name/path substring matching; Cascade also supports `*` wildcards and quoted phrases there in most versions). This repo doesn't document or enforce that format beyond forwarding the string, so the authoritative answer is your Cascade instance's search behavior, not something in this code.

For `smoke_test.py` specifically: just pick a plain substring likely to appear in an asset name/path on your dev site (e.g. part of a known page's filename), paired with a `SITE` that has at least one data-bound page, since Phase 2's tools need a `contentTypeId` reference to fire.

Sonnet 5 · Auto · 2026-09-02 23:42 UTC
Two separate confusions to untangle here:

**You don't need to search for the data definition at all.** `cascade_get_data_structure` takes the *same* `identifier` you're already using for `cascade_read_asset`/`cascade_get_page_config` — the page's own identifier. Internally it does the `contentTypeId` → `contenttype` asset → `dataDefinitionId` resolution chain itself (`resolution.py`); you never look up or path-search the Data Definitions folder yourself. That's the whole point of Phase 2 being schema-authoritative rather than sampling — the tool walks the reference chain for you.

**The only real unknown is the `group` identifier** — a group name from inside that data definition's XML schema, which you don't know ahead of time. But you don't need to discover it by hand either: the tool's error path is designed exactly for this. Call it with any (even wrong) `group` name, and `errors.group_not_found_error` returns a `ToolError` listing the real available group identifiers, sorted by relevance to your guess. That's the intended discovery path, not `cascade_search`.

Let me wire that "probe → read the error → retry with a real group" pattern into the script so it's self-driving:

That change explains it — you redirected `QUERY` to search for the "Data Definitions" folder itself, but that's not how this works. `identifier` throughout the script must stay a **page** (or other content-bound) asset — the same one from the original search — because `cascade_get_data_structure` resolves the data definition *from* that asset's `contentTypeId`, not from a separately-found data-definition asset. Searching for "Data Definitions" gets you a folder, which has no `contentTypeId`, so `content_type_ref` would be `None` and the whole block gets skipped anyway.

Searching for the "Data Definitions" folder directly breaks the chain (a folder has no `contentTypeId`).

2. **Replaced the placeholder `group="<real-group-identifier>"`** (which would've thrown an unhandled `ToolError` and crashed the script) **with a probe step**: it calls `cascade_get_data_structure` with a deliberately bogus group name (`"__probe__"`), catches the resulting `ToolError`, and prints it — that error message lists every real group identifier available on this content type's data definition (that's what `group_not_found_error` in `errors.py` is designed for).

Not a library bug — this is expected behavior the smoke test didn't account for. `accordion`'s children are `row` (tag `group`), `show` (tag `text`), `type` (tag `text`) — `list_children` mixes group and leaf entries together in one flat directory listing (by design), but `find_node` only ever returns **leaf** fields (any non-`group` tag); it deliberately never returns a group itself as a node, and descends into `row` looking for *another* child literally named `"row"`, which doesn't exist. Hence the error.

The script picked `children[0]` blindly without checking `tag`, and `row` happened to sort first alphabetically. To drill into a nested group like `row`, you call `cascade_get_data_structure(group="row")` directly — `find_group` searches the whole tree by identifier regardless of nesting depth, so no path is needed.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   - Continuing an incremental, phase-by-phase implementation of an MCP (Model Context Protocol) server (`src/cascade_cms/mcp/`) wrapping the `cascade_cms` Python library, per the user's standing instruction from earlier in the session: "The actual implementation will be a incremental process. I want you to pause and ask for at each step."
   - This session picked up after Phase 1 (`cascade_search`, `cascade_read_asset`) and Phase 2 (`cascade_get_data_structure`, `cascade_get_page_config`) were already implemented and unit-tested (167 tests passing). The user's immediate goals in this session were:
     a. Understand the `QUERY` string format accepted by `cascade_search` (asked directly)
     b. Manually run `tests/mcp/smoke_test.py` against their real dev Cascade site (`APPTEST` server, `[redacted: internal-host]:8443`) to validate Phase 2 end-to-end, iterating through several rounds of "I ran it, here's the output" / "it errored" exchanges until the script ran clean
     c. "Let's move on to Phase 3" — implement `cascade_root_container_id` + `cascade_list_sites` per `MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_REV.md` §6.5/§6.6
     d. Mid-Phase-3-planning, explicit instruction: "Check in with the other agent working on a different issue `drop-asset-type-key-aliases`. See if it conflicts with your changes." — a cross-session coordination check before finalizing the plan
   - Standing constraint carried forward from earlier in the session (not repeated this session, but never countermanded): nothing should be `git commit`-ed without explicit user instruction. No commits have been made.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MCP tool functions in this repo** (`src/cascade_cms/mcp/server.py`) are plain Python functions decorated with `@mcp.tool()`, callable directly (bypassing the MCP protocol's dict-to-Pydantic coercion) for manual smoke testing.
   - **`cascade_search`'s `query` param** maps directly, unvalidated, to `SearchInformation.searchTerms` (`cmstypes.py`), sent as-is to Cascade's REST `search` endpoint (`operations.py:429-438`). No parsing/escaping happens in this library — multi-word/quoted search syntax is entirely Cascade server-side behavior.
   - **`cascade_get_data_structure`'s discovery model**: callers never search for or directly address a data-definition asset. They pass the SAME `identifier` used for `cascade_read_asset`/`cascade_get_page_config` (e.g. a page), and the tool internally resolves `contentTypeId` → `contenttype` asset → `dataDefinitionId` via `resolution.py`. The only unknown param is `group` — discoverable via probing with a bogus value and reading the self-correcting `ToolError` from `errors.group_not_found_error`, which lists every real, sorted group identifier via `formatting.format_names_for_message`/`sort_by_relevance`.
   - **`data_structure.find_group`** does a recursive descent over the WHOLE XML tree matching by identifier regardless of nesting depth — so a nested group is reachable via a fresh top-level `group=<that identifier>` call, no path syntax needed.
   - **`data_structure.find_node`** only ever returns a LEAF field (any non-`group` tag); it never returns a `group`-tagged node itself, and recurses into nested groups looking for a same-named leaf elsewhere. Meanwhile **`list_children`** (used for the group listing response) mixes group and leaf entries together in one flat, alphabetically-sorted directory listing — callers must check each entry's `"tag"` field before choosing whether to pass its identifier as `node_identifier` (a leaf) or as a fresh `group=` call (a nested group).
   - **Cross-session coordination via `ListAgents`/`SendMessage`**: `ListAgents` enumerates peer Claude Code sessions on the machine (and elsewhere); `SendMessage(to: "<peer-name>", message: "...")` is the correct way to contact an existing peer session — the `Agent` tool (even with `subagent_type: "claude"`) spawns a BRAND NEW, context-free agent and does NOT reach an existing named peer session. This distinction was a real mistake I made and had to self-correct (see Errors section).
   - **`Asset.internal_type`** (cmstypes.py): renamed from `Asset.asset_type` by a concurrent peer session's change (`drop-asset-type-key-aliases`, already landed on the shared working tree). Returns the raw, lowercased Cascade response-wrapper key with NO normalization (the old `_ASSET_TYPE_KEY_ALIASES` dict, which only ever mapped `"scriptformat"`→`"format"`, was removed entirely). For "site" assets this is an identity re-case either way, so `.internal_type == "site"` behaves identically to the old `.asset_type == "site"`.
   - **`Asset.root_container_id(asset_type)`** (cmstypes.py, UNTOUCHED by the peer's change): requires `self` to be a `site` asset; looks up one of three known fields via `_ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS` (`"datadefinition"`→`"rootDataDefinitionContainerId"`, `"sharedfield"`→`"rootSharedFieldContainerId"`, `"folder"`→`"rootFolderId"`), returns `uuid.UUID | None`.
   - **`Operations.listSites()`** (operations.py:471-475): GET with no payload, parsed via `parse_list_elements` into `ListElements` whose `elements` are `IdentifierType` objects aliased from the `"sites"` JSON key (confirmed directly in the `ListElements` model's `AliasChoices`).
   - **Bare-hex UUID serialization convention**: this codebase consistently serializes Cascade UUIDs as 32-char lowercase hex with no dashes (e.g. `IdentifierType.serialize_identifier` uses `.hex`), not Python's default dashed `str(uuid)`.
   - **Defensive truncation pattern** (established Phase 2, reused Phase 3): `formatting.truncate_list(items, key=..., limit=..., expand_with=...)` for successful listings (dict-shaped, `total_count`/`has_more`); `formatting.format_names_for_message`/`sort_by_relevance` for error-message listings.

       ```python
       try:
           cascade_get_data_structure(identifier=identifier, group="__probe__")
           group_name = None
       except ToolError as exc:
           print(f"[probe] {exc}")
           match = re.search(r"Available groups: (.+)\.$", str(exc))
           names = re.sub(r", and \d+ more$", "", match.group(1)) if match else ""
           group_name = names.split(", ")[0] if names else None

       if group_name:
           groups = cascade_get_data_structure(identifier=identifier, group=group_name)
           print(groups)
           leaf = next(
               (c for c in groups["children"] if c["tag"] != "group"), None
           )
           if leaf:
               node = cascade_get_data_structure(
                   identifier=identifier, group=group_name, node_identifier=leaf["identifier"]
               )
               print(node)
       ```

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/errors.py`** (extended, Phase 3)
     - Added three new constructors, inserted after `page_region_requires_configuration_name_error` and before `unexpected_failure_error`:
       ```python
       def not_a_site_error(asset: Asset, *, context: str) -> ToolError:
           return ToolError(
               f"{context}: expected a site asset, but read a '{asset.internal_type}' asset "
               f"({asset.get('name')!r}) instead. Root container ids only exist on site assets - "
               "pass the identifier/path of the site itself."
           )

       def no_root_container_error(site: Asset, asset_type: str, *, context: str) -> ToolError:
           available = format_names_for_message(sorted(Asset._ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS.keys()))
           return ToolError(
               f"{context}: '{asset_type}' has no known root container field on site "
               f"{site.get('name')!r}. Supported asset_type values: {available}."
           )

       def list_sites_failure_error(result: CascadeError | Exception) -> ToolError:
           if isinstance(result, CascadeError):
               message = (
                   result.message
                   or "Cascade reported a listSites failure with no further detail."
               )
               return ToolError(f"cascade_list_sites failed: {message}")
           return ToolError(
               f"cascade_list_sites failed unexpectedly ({result}). This usually indicates a "
               "connectivity/credential problem - check CASCADE_URL and CASCADE_API_KEY."
           )
       ```
     - Used `Asset.internal_type` (not `.asset_type`) per the peer session's confirmed rename.
     - Ruff-formatted after initial edit (function signatures collapsed to one line where they fit).

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/server.py`** (extended, Phase 3)
     - Added two new `@mcp.tool()` functions before `main()`:
       ```python
       @mcp.tool()
       def cascade_root_container_id(
           site_identifier: IdentifierType | CascadePath,
           asset_type: Literal["datadefinition", "sharedfield", "folder"],
       ) -> dict[str, Any]:
           """Get the root container id (top-level folder) for datadefinition/sharedfield/
           folder assets on a site - the starting point for browsing that asset type's
           tree from the top (e.g. the site's root Data Definitions folder)."""
           try:
               with _wrapper() as cascade:
                   cascade.operations.read(site_identifier)
                   result = errors.single_result(
                       cascade.submit_requests(), context="cascade_root_container_id"
                   )

               if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception):
                   raise errors.read_asset_error(
                       site_identifier,
                       result,
                       context="cascade_root_container_id",
                       purpose="the site",
                   )

               assert isinstance(result, Asset)
               if result.internal_type != "site":
                   raise errors.not_a_site_error(result, context="cascade_root_container_id")

               container_id = result.root_container_id(cast(AssetTypes, asset_type))
               if container_id is None:
                   raise errors.no_root_container_error(
                       result, asset_type, context="cascade_root_container_id"
                   )
               return {"container_id": container_id.hex}
           except ToolError:
               raise
           except Exception as exc:
               raise errors.unexpected_failure_error(
                   "cascade_root_container_id", exc
               ) from exc

       @mcp.tool()
       def cascade_list_sites(limit: int | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
           """List every site on this Cascade server."""
           try:
               with _wrapper() as cascade:
                   cascade.operations.listSites()
                   result = errors.single_result(
                       cascade.submit_requests(), context="cascade_list_sites"
                   )

               if isinstance(result, CascadeError | Exception):
                   raise errors.list_sites_failure_error(result)

               assert isinstance(result, ListElements)
               sites = [
                   formatting.describe_element(e)
                   for e in result.flat
                   if isinstance(e, IdentifierType)
               ]
               return formatting.truncate_list(
                   sites, key="sites", limit=limit or formatting.DEFAULT_LIST_LIMIT
               )
           except ToolError:
               raise
           except Exception as exc:
               raise errors.unexpected_failure_error("cascade_list_sites", exc) from exc
       ```
     - Ruff-formatted after initial edit.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/src/cascade_cms/mcp/formatting.py`** (modified)
     - Renamed `_describe_element` → `describe_element` (dropped leading underscore, made public) via `sed`, since Phase 3's `cascade_list_sites` in `server.py` needed to call it cross-module — a leading-underscore name is module-private by convention, so this matches the existing precedent of other cross-reused helpers (`truncate_list`, `format_names_for_message`, `sort_by_relevance`) being public. Both the definition (line ~72) and the internal usage inside `format_search_results` (line ~94) were updated.

     - `_ASSET_TYPE_KEY_ALIASES` removed entirely.
     - `Asset.asset_type` property renamed to `Asset.internal_type` (confirmed live on disk at line 622):
       ```python
       @property
       def internal_type(self) -> str:
           """The raw Cascade response wrapper key, lowercased.

           Not a validated `AssetTypes` value - Cascade's wrapper key doesn't
           always match the request-side type (e.g. `"scriptformat"` for a
           `format`/`format_SCRIPT` asset). This is only ever round-tripped back
           into a request body (see `AssetAdapter.dump_json`), which is why no
           normalization happens here; callers that need a validated
           `AssetTypes` must supply/derive one themselves.
           """
           return self._asset_type.lower()
       ```
     - `Asset._ROOT_CONTAINER_FIELDS` and `Asset.root_container_id()` confirmed UNCHANGED at lines 725-743.
     - `identifier_from_asset()` renamed to `edit_log_identifier_from_asset()`, now returns a lightweight `AssetLogIdentifier` NamedTuple instead of a strict `IdentifierType` — confirmed by the peer as irrelevant to Phase 3's MCP tools.

   - **`/mnt/D/py-cascade-cms/tests/mcp/test_server.py`** (extended, Phase 3 — 8 new tests appended at end of file)
     ```python
     def _site_asset(**overrides) -> Asset:
         data = {
             "id": uuid.uuid4().hex,
             "name": "example-site",
             "rootDataDefinitionContainerId": uuid.uuid4().hex,
             "rootSharedFieldContainerId": uuid.uuid4().hex,
             "rootFolderId": uuid.uuid4().hex,
         }
         data.update(overrides)
         return Asset({"asset": {"site": data}})

     def test_cascade_root_container_id_returns_hex_id(patch_wrapper):
         site = _site_asset()
         patch_wrapper([site])
         result = server.cascade_root_container_id(
             site_identifier=_identifier(), asset_type="folder"
         )
         assert result == {"container_id": site.get("rootFolderId")}

     def test_cascade_root_container_id_not_a_site_raises(patch_wrapper):
         page = Asset({"asset": {"page": {"id": uuid.uuid4().hex, "name": "not-a-site"}}})
         patch_wrapper([page])
         with pytest.raises(ToolError, match="expected a site asset"):
             server.cascade_root_container_id(
                 site_identifier=_identifier(), asset_type="folder"
             )

     def test_cascade_root_container_id_unmapped_type_raises(patch_wrapper):
         site = _site_asset()
         del site._data["rootFolderId"]
         patch_wrapper([site])
         with pytest.raises(ToolError, match="folder"):
             server.cascade_root_container_id(
                 site_identifier=_identifier(), asset_type="folder"
             )

     def test_cascade_root_container_id_raises_tool_error_on_cascade_error(patch_wrapper):
         patch_wrapper([CascadeError(success=False, message="Site not found")])
         with pytest.raises(ToolError, match="Site not found"):
             server.cascade_root_container_id(
                 site_identifier=_identifier(), asset_type="folder"
             )

     def test_cascade_list_sites_returns_formatted_sites(patch_wrapper):
         elements = ListElements.model_validate(
             {
                 "sites": [
                     IdentifierType(
                         identifier=uuid.uuid4(), asset_type="site", path={"path": ""}
                     ).model_dump(by_alias=True)
                 ]
             }
         )
         patch_wrapper([elements])
         result = server.cascade_list_sites()
         assert result["total_count"] == 1
         assert result["has_more"] is False
         assert result["sites"][0]["type"] == "site"

     def test_cascade_list_sites_truncates_with_limit(patch_wrapper):
         raw = [
             IdentifierType(
                 identifier=uuid.uuid4(), asset_type="site", path={"path": ""}
             ).model_dump(by_alias=True)
             for _ in range(5)
         ]
         elements = ListElements.model_validate({"sites": raw})
         patch_wrapper([elements])
         result = server.cascade_list_sites(limit=2)
         assert result["total_count"] == 5
         assert result["has_more"] is True
         assert len(result["sites"]) == 2

     def test_cascade_list_sites_raises_tool_error_on_cascade_error(patch_wrapper):
         patch_wrapper([CascadeError(success=False, message="listSites unavailable")])
         with pytest.raises(ToolError, match="listSites unavailable"):
             server.cascade_list_sites()
     ```
     - `patch_wrapper` here is the pre-existing single-shot `_FakeWrapper` fixture already defined in `test_server.py` (distinct from `patch_wrapper_sequence` in `conftest.py`, which is for multi-hop chains — not needed here since both new tools do exactly one `submit_requests()` call).

Final approved content: Context section (including the concurrent-session coordination paragraph naming `drop-asset-type-key-aliases` and the `internal_type` rename), Design section for both tools (exact param types, error paths, formatting reuse), Files tree, Verification section. This is the plan I actually implemented against.

   - **Script picked a nested group instead of a leaf field, causing `field 'row' not found in group 'accordion'`**: Root-caused (not a library bug) by reading `find_node`'s exact implementation — it never returns a `group`-tagged child as a match, only recurses into it looking for a same-named leaf. The script had blindly used `groups["children"][0]["identifier"]`, which happened to be a nested `group` ("row"), not a leaf. Fixed by filtering for `c["tag"] != "group"` before picking a `node_identifier` candidate. Verified working on next real run (returned the "show" field's full radiobutton definition).

I checked the current state before planning Phase 4, and there are two real architectural forks the plan doc itself flags as "confirm with Keith" — worth resolving before writing anything.

**Already done:** `mcp>=2.1.0,<3` is already in `dev-dependencies` in `pyproject.toml` (landed in an earlier commit) — that part of Phase 4 is complete.

**Open items I found:**

1. **Where `mcp/` source lives.** It currently sits at `src/cascade_cms/mcp/`. But `[tool.hatch.build.targets.wheel] packages = ["src/cascade_cms"]` means anything under `src/cascade_cms/` ships inside the published `cascade-cms-rest` wheel — so as-is, `mcp/` would get bundled into every normal `pip install cascade-cms-rest`, even though the `mcp` SDK itself stays a dev-only dependency. The plan's own §3 says `mcp/` should be a **sibling to `skill/`**, not a subpackage of `cascade_cms` — which would mean moving it out of `src/` entirely (e.g. to a repo-root `mcp/` folder), at the cost of losing the clean `cascade_cms.mcp` import path during development.

2. **Release mechanics.** I checked how `skill/` is actually distributed: `build_skill.py` is a manual, non-CI step (confirmed in `AGENTS.md`) that zips a bundle into a versioned file (e.g. `skill/cascade-script-writer-311.skill`) which then gets **committed directly into the repo** — `.github/workflows/release.yml` never touches `skill/` at all; it only builds/publishes the `sdist`/`wheel` to PyPI on a tag push. So "GitHub Release artifact mirroring `skill/`" in practice means: a similar build script producing a versioned zip, committed the same way — not new CI automation.

Before I start moving files — there's a real Python packaging landmine in the "repo-root `mcp/`" choice, worth flagging before I touch anything.

The MCP SDK's own top-level import name **is literally `mcp`** (`from mcp.server.mcpserver import MCPServer`, etc. — that's the installed `mcp>=2.1.0,<3` dev dependency). If our own package also becomes a bare `mcp/` folder at the repo root, then any time the repo root ends up on `sys.path` — running `python mcp/server.py` directly, running `smoke_test.py` from the repo root, pytest's rootdir insertion — our local folder would **shadow the real SDK package**, and `from mcp.server.mcpserver import MCPServer` inside our own `server.py` would try to import from itself instead of the SDK. That's not a style nitpick, it would break at runtime in exactly the way `skill/`'s folder (named `cascade-script-writer`, not `skill`) never risks, since nothing there collides with an installed dependency's import name.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Build the cascade-cms-rest-mcp release artifact from tracked source.

Zips this directory's installable project (pyproject.toml, README.md,
src/cascade_cms_rest_mcp/) into a versioned archive under dist/, committed
into the repo the same way skill/*.skill files are - not published to PyPI,
not wired into CI. An end user downloads the zip, unpacks it, and points
`uvx --from <unpacked-dir> cascade-cms-rest-mcp` at it (see README.md).

    python mcp/build_release.py                  # version from pyproject.toml
    python mcp/build_release.py --version 0.2.0
    python mcp/build_release.py --no-zip          # validate only, skip the zip
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import re
import sys
import zipfile
from pathlib import Path

MCP_DIR = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
DIST_DIR = MCP_DIR / "dist"

# Everything a downstream `uvx --from <unpacked-dir> cascade-cms-rest-mcp`
# needs to resolve dependencies and find the console-script entry point.
INCLUDE = ("pyproject.toml", "README.md", "src")


def read_version() -> str:
    text = (MCP_DIR / "pyproject.toml").read_text()
    match = re.search(r'^version\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"', text, re.MULTILINE)
    if not match:
        sys.exit("[BUILD ERROR] Could not find version in mcp/pyproject.toml")
    return match.group(1)


def collect_files() -> list[Path]:
    files: list[Path] = []
    for name in INCLUDE:
        source = MCP_DIR / name
        if source.is_file():
            files.append(source)
        elif source.is_dir():
            for path in sorted(source.rglob("*")):
                if path.is_dir() or "__pycache__" in path.parts:
                    continue
                files.append(path)
        else...

# cascade-cms-rest-mcp

A local, read-only [MCP](https://modelcontextprotocol.io) server exposing a
Hannon Hill Cascade CMS server to MCP clients (Claude Desktop, Claude Code,
etc.), built on top of [`cascade-cms-rest`](https://pypi.org/project/cascade-cms-rest/).

Seven tools, all read-only — no tool in this server can perform a write
operation, even indirectly:

| Tool | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `cascade_search` | Search a site for assets by text |
| `cascade_read_asset` | Read a single asset by id or by site+path |
| `cascade_get_data_structure` | A data-bound asset's field schema, resolved from its bound content type/data definition |
| `cascade_get_page_config` | A data-bound asset's page configuration names/regions |
| `cascade_root_container_id` | The root container id (e.g. Data Definitions folder) for an asset type on a site |
| `cascade_list_sites` | List every site on the server |

## Known limitation

`cascade_get_data_structure` and `cascade_get_page_config` currently report
field/group/config names by sampling the *one asset instance* you point them
at — not the full schema-valid set the asset's type actually allows. See
`MCP_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_REV.md` §7 in the `py-cascade-cms` repo for the
deferred schema-authoritative design this is expected to move to.

## Configuration

Required environment variables (same names `CascadeWrapperBase` already
expects — no new credential-naming surface):

| Variable | Required | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `CASCADE_API_KEY` | Yes | Cascade API key |
| `CASCADE_URL` | Yes | e.g. `https://your-cascade-host:8443` |
| `SERVER` | No | Cosmetic — log-file naming, defaults to `default` |
| `CASCADE_MCP_CACHE_DIR` | No | Overrides the default `~/.cache/cascade-cms-mcp` response-c...

import hashlib

import subprocess
import sys
import zipfile
from pathlib import Path

REPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent
SKILL_DIR = REPO_ROOT / "skill\""""

old_read_version = '''def read_version() -> str:
    text = (REPO_ROOT / "pyproject.toml").read_text()
    match = re.search(r'^version\\s*=\\s*"([^"]+)"', text, re.MULTILINE)
    if not match:
        sys.exit("[BUILD ERROR] Could not find version in pyproject.toml")
    return match.group(1)'''

new_read_version = '''def read_version() -> str:
    """cascade-cms-tools has no local copy of cascade_cms's source (see
    sync_snapshot below) - the installed library's own version is the only
    correct source of truth for what a freshly-built bundle actually bundles.
    """
    try:
        return importlib.metadata.version("cascade-cms-rest")
    except importlib.metadata.PackageNotFoundError:
        sys.exit(
            "[BUILD ERROR] cascade-cms-rest is not installed in this environment - "
            "run `pip install cascade-cms-rest` (or `--upgrade` to bundle a newly-"
            "released version) before building."
        )'''

assert old_read_version in text, "old_read_version block not fou...

# No [project]/[build-system] here on purpose - nothing is built or installed
# from the repo root itself. mcp/ is its own installable project
# (mcp/pyproject.toml); skill/ is not a Python package at all. This file only
# carries tool config shared across both.

[tool.ruff]
# Skill bundle contents are not library code: cascade_cms/ (synced from the
# installed cascade-cms-rest package by skill/build_skill.py) is a vendored
# snapshot, and templates/ and scripts/ are standalone files that ship to end
# users - only the builders at skill/*.py are linted.
exclude = [
    "skill/cascade-script-writer",
    "skill/cascade-script-writer-lite",
    # Manual human-run script against a live Cascade instance (see its own
    # docstring) - not library code or an automated test, so lint rules aimed
    # at production code don't apply. mcp/tests/conftest.py also excludes it
    # from pytest collection, since its *_test.py-adjacent name would
    # otherwise make pytest import (and execute) it as a test module.
    "mcp/tests/smoke_test.py",
]
EOF

        with:
          python-version: ${{ matrix.python-version }}
      - name: Install package
        run: pip install -e "./mcp[dev]"
      - name: Lint
        run: ruff check .

      - name: Test

cat > /mnt/D/cascade-cms-tools/README.md << 'EOF'
# cascade-cms-tools

Tooling built on top of [`cascade-cms-rest`](https://github.com/Sharkdroid/py-cascade-cms)
(the Hannon Hill Cascade CMS REST client library) — kept in its own repo so
the library itself stays a clean, dependency-light package.

| Directory | What it is |
|---|---|
| [`mcp/`](mcp/) | A local, read-only MCP server exposing a Cascade CMS server to MCP clients (Claude Desktop, Claude Code, etc.) |
| [`skill/`](skill/) | `cascade-script-writer`/`cascade-script-writer-lite` — Claude Code skills for writing scripts against `cascade-cms-rest` |

Both depend on the **published** `cascade-cms-rest` package (PyPI), not a
vendored copy — see each directory's own docs for how it's kept in sync.

## Development

```bash
pip install -e "./mcp[dev]"
pytest
ruff check .
mypy mcp/src/
```

See `AGENTS.md` for the skill-authoring workflow and how to rebuild release
artifacts for both `mcp/` and `skill/`.
EOF
echo written

# AGENTS.md

`cascade-cms-tools` — the MCP server and script-writing skills for
[`cascade-cms-rest`](https://github.com/Sharkdroid/py-cascade-cms). Both
depend on the *published* `cascade-cms-rest` package; neither vendors a copy
of the library's source.

## Repo commands

```bash
pip install -e "./mcp[dev]"
pytest
ruff check .
mypy mcp/src/
```

CI runs all three. Run them before considering a change done.

## Writing scripts that use cascade-cms-rest

Two skills, same validator, same library snapshot:

| Skill | Use it for |
|---|---|
| `skill/cascade-script-writer/` | Multi-operation pipelines, callback patterns, anything no template covers. 21 templates, full JSON schema. |
| `skill/cascade-script-writer-lite/` | Single-operation scripts, and any small/fast model. 6 templates, one generated `references/OPS.md`, ~3.5k tokens to read instead of ~16k. |

The lite skill forbids reading `cascade_cms/*.py` — that snapshot is there for
the validator to import, not to be used as documentation. Its `OPS.md` is
generated by `skill/gen_ops_reference.py`; never hand-edit it.

Start from a template:

```bash
cd skill/cascade-script-writer
python scripts/new_script.py --list
python scripts/new_script.py --template create-bulk --out my_script.py
```

Then validate:

```bash
python skill/cascade-script-writer/scripts/validate_script.py my_script.py
```

**A generated script is complete only when `validate_script.py` exits 0.** Fix
what it reports and re-run; cap at 3 attempts, then surface the output verbatim
rather than looping.

Two rules the validator enforces and scripts must never break:

- `CascadeWrapperBase` is the only entry point — no `cascade_cms.driver`, no
  manual asyncio event loop, no hand-built `aiohttp.ClientSessio...

.venv/bin/pip install -e "/mnt/D/cascade-cms-tools/mcp[dev]" 2>&1 | tail -20

Sonnet 5 · Auto · 2026-09-03 01:35 UTC
Good question — let me walk through what each builder actually needs.

**`mcp/build_release.py`**: Purely mechanical. It just zips `pyproject.toml`, `README.md`, and `src/` as they exist in the checked-out tree. No external checks, no possible failure mode short of a missing file. Zero intervention needed, ever.

**`skill/build_skill.py`**: Every step is also mechanical — sync the installed library, write a manifest, regenerate the lite skill's `OPS.md`, check size budgets, run `validate_script.py` against each template, zip. It runs start-to-finish with no human/agent in the loop, and I verified this directly a moment ago (21/21 templates validated in one unattended run).

The line in `AGENTS.md` — "not CI automation, and never will be" — isn't about the script needing supervision to *run*. It's about what happens *if `validate_templates()` fails*: that means a `cascade-cms-rest` release changed an API a template relies on, and the **fix** (rewriting that template correctly) needs judgment no script has. But that's identical in shape to any other CI gate — mypy failing, a test failing — the build just fails loudly and blocks the release. Nothing about a failure requires an agent to be *present* for the CI run itself; it just means someone fixes it before tagging again.

So: yes, this can be fully automated. I read the original phrasing as stronger than it should've been — I'll soften that `AGENTS.md` section to describe validation failure as a normal CI gate rather than implying the build step itself resists automation.

That leaves one open call: the repo currently keeps every past build committed simultaneously (`cascade-script-writer-203.skill`, `-301.skill`, `-311.skill` all present at once). Should a release attach only the newest artifact per bundle (3 assets: latest skill, latest lite-skill, latest mcp zip), or every version currently tracked?


Keith · 2026-09-03 01:37 UTC

`.github/workflows/release.yml` is added and fully verified — I simulated a clean checkout (fresh copy, no leftover local state) and ran every step: unpack, `ruff check .`, `mypy mcp/src/`, `pytest` (87 passed), `build_skill.py --no-zip` (21 + 6 templates validated), `build_release.py --no-zip`. All green.

**What it does**, on any `v*.*.*` tag push:
1. `validate` job — installs from `mcp/pyproject.toml`, unpacks the newest committed `cascade-script-writer-*.skill`/`-lite-*.skill` (their hand-authored content only exists inside those zips, not as tracked source), runs lint/typecheck/tests, then re-syncs and re-validates both skill bundles against whatever `cascade-cms-rest` version just got installed, plus a packaging sanity check for `mcp/`. Any failure blocks the release — same as any other CI gate.
2. `release` job (needs `validate`) — creates the GitHub release for the tag and attaches **every** currently-tracked `skill/*.skill` and `mcp/dist/*.zip`, per your call to keep full version history in each release rather than just the latest.

It never runs `build_skill.py`/`build_release.py` *without* `--no-zip` — it publishes whatever's already committed, it doesn't generate new artifacts. So the workflow is: rebuild+commit locally whichever of `skill/`/`mcp/` changed, then tag and push.

I also softened the `AGENTS.md` language that said CI automation "will never" run the skill build — that was conflating "the build script needs supervision to run" (it doesn't) with "fixing a validation failure needs judgment" (it does). Added a "Cutting a release" section spelling out the tag → validate → publish flow.

Nothing is committed yet in either repo (still your call on when/order). One thing to flag: while testing the workflow logic I ran `git add -A` in `cascade-cms-tools` as part of a stash-based simulation setup, so its `git status` now shows everything staged rather than untracked — no content changed, just stage state; let me know if you'd rather I unstage it back to how it was.

Before I push — heads up: `py-cascade-cms` has its own `.github/workflows/release.yml` that triggers on any `v*.*.*` tag push and **publishes to PyPI**. Pushing both `v3.1.2` and `v3.1.3` will kick off two separate PyPI releases — including `3.1.2`, which only ever existed as an intermediate step to cleanly separate the changelog entries, and would be live on PyPI (and effectively immediately superseded) moments later. PyPI releases can't be deleted, only yanked.
